### Page 15

A growing part of AI ﬁnancing is moving oﬀ balance sheet. Special
purpose vehicles and joint ventures, such as Meta's Hyperion data
centre ﬁnanced with Blue Owl, long-term leases and GPU-backed
loans allow companies to fund the build-out without the debt
appearing directly on their own books. Securitisation of data centre
assets through asset-backed securities and commercial mortgage-
backed securities is growing as well, bringing a wider pool of

investors into the game.
Leases are a particular blind spot. They are recognised as on-
balance-sheet commitments only once contracts are ﬁnal; until
then, the extent of incremental lease liabilities is uncertain. There is
also the question of who carries the risk if the tenant walks away
after the initial term. Although initial lease terms of 15 years are
not uncommon, these can still be shorter than the economic life of
the underlying infrastructure, leaving some residual risk around

renewal or re-leasing.
That said, risk has to be assessed contract by contract. The largest
technology ﬁrms, with very solid balance sheets, are well placed to
take on additional liabilities, and much of the oﬀ-balance-sheet
structure ultimately rests on their creditworthiness. The problem is
less hidden insolvency than hidden concentration: it is hard to see
how much of the system leans on the same handful of tenants.
Opacity also complicates the macro picture. When ﬁnancing is

spread across SPVs, private credit funds and securitisations, it
becomes harder for investors, regulators and central banks to see
where risks are concentrated – and harder to judge how a shock
would travel.
Looking ahead, capital may eventually become a major constraint
on the AI build-out, alongside permitting and access to power. The
more the industry relies on creative ﬁnancing, the more it depends
on investors' continued willingness to lend. Financial engineering

can prolong a boom, but it can't make the returns appear.
How exposed are banks, and is private
credit the shadow banking of this cycle?

### Page 16

Direct bank exposure looks modest; private credit is the obvious
weak spot – but contagion would run through markets rather than
bank failures.
Banks' direct exposure to AI looks limited. The hyperscalers fund
themselves mainly through cash ﬂow and bond markets rather
than bank loans. Indirect exposure is harder to gauge: banks lend to
private credit funds, data centre developers and construction ﬁrms.
Private credit is the obvious weak spot. Is it the shadow banking of

this cycle? Partly. It has less of the maturity mismatch that made
pre-2008 shadow banking so fragile: much of the capital is locked
up in closed-end funds, so investors can't run. But it shares other
features: opacity, infrequent valuation marks and leverage on
leverage, with funds borrowing from banks to lend to borrowers
that are themselves highly leveraged. And it is increasingly exposed
to AI through loans to neoclouds, GPU-backed ﬁnancing and data

centre developers.
The pressure point is the semi-liquid vehicles sold to retail and
wealth clients. They promise periodic liquidity on assets that are
anything but liquid. In a downturn, redemption requests could force
managers to impose gates, damaging conﬁdence across the asset
class. So far, these vehicles are not core to the AI build-out, but they
are where a shock could turn into a story.
If stress does materialise, we would expect contagion to run

through tighter ﬁnancial conditions and wider credit spreads across
the economy rather than through bank failures. That is less
dramatic than 2008, but not harmless: a repricing of credit risk
would raise funding costs far beyond tech, hitting the very non-tech
investment that is already struggling.
Banks may well escape an AI bust largely unscathed. The economy
they lend to may not.
Vendor ﬁnancing déjà vu: should we worry
when chipmakers fund their own

customers?

### Page 17

Worth watching, but this is not Lucent and Nortel all over again –
mainly because the balance sheets are much stronger.
The dotcom precedent is uncomfortable. Lucent, Nortel and Cisco
lent money to telecom start-ups so they could buy their
equipment. When the customers went bust, the suppliers took the
hit, and much of the reported demand turned out to have been
ﬁnanced by the sellers themselves.
Today, there are echoes. Chipmakers are taking stakes in AI labs

and neoclouds that then buy their chips. The same handful of ﬁrms
raise money, buy chips, lease compute and book revenue oﬀ one
another. The concern is less solvency than signal: circular deals
inﬂate reported demand and blur true end-demand. Revenue that
only exists because the supplier paid for it isn't demand.
But the picture diﬀers in important ways. Many of these contracts
appear to be normal commercial arrangements, and AI labs also
generate fast-growing revenues from customers outside these

related deals. AI-related revenues are still only part of the much
larger businesses of the hyperscalers. And the macro backdrop is
very diﬀerent: the overall US corporate debt-to-GDP ratio is not
worrisome, in stark contrast to the leverage that built up before the
dotcom bust and the global ﬁnancial crisis. Circularity in a cash-rich
system is a transparency problem; circularity in a leveraged system
is a solvency problem.
That does not mean investors can relax. Circular deals create

concentration risk: if one key AI lab stumbles, the shock would
travel quickly through its suppliers, landlords and investors, who are
often the same companies. And they make it harder to judge how
much genuine end-demand there is for AI – precisely the question
on which the whole boom rests.
The test is simple: follow the money until it reaches a customer
who isn't also an investor. The longer that trail gets, the more
nervous we should become.
Higher Fed rates helped end the dotcom

bubble. Could monetary policy or bond

### Page 18

yields pop this one too?
Probably not as the trigger – but monetary policy could make any
bust more painful, because the safety net is thinner than in 2001.
In 1999-2000, the Fed hiked rates to 6.5% before the dotcom
bubble burst, then cut by almost ﬁve percentage points in 2001.
This time, an energy shock is keeping inﬂation above target. Actual
rate hikes and more rate hikes to come could bring both the Federal
Reserve and European Central Bank closer to risky territory.

High policy rates do not necessarily stop AI investment ﬁrst. If
expected returns on AI remain very high, capital may keep ﬂowing
in while ﬁnancing becomes uneconomic for lower-return
investment elsewhere. Tight money could deepen the split between
tech and non-tech investment rather than end the boom.
At the moment, we expect hyperscaler funding needs to increase
further as investment in AI infrastructure, data centres and power
capacity accelerates. We forecast total issuance of around

US$280bn in 2027, including approximately US$190bn in USD and
US$65bn in EUR (€60bn). For European credit markets, we estimate
a net TMT supply number of €103bn, which includes US$65bn
coming from hyperscalers.
The long end of the yield curve matters more than the policy rate.
As the AI build-out becomes increasingly debt-funded, higher bond
yields and term premia raise the cost of ﬁnancing long-term
investments, from data centres to power plants. A sustained rise in

long-term yields would therefore hit hardest where the build-out
depends most on external funding.
The bigger issue is the safety net. If the bubble bursts, central banks
can't come to the rescue as aggressively as in 2001 while inﬂation
is above target. Fiscal space is also far more limited than a quarter
of a century ago.
But don't rule out a diﬀerent kind of rescue. AI is not just an
economic tool; it is also about power – geopolitical and
technological. If things went south for the sector, some form of

government support would be conceivable, from public

procurement to guarantees or targeted support for strategically

Monetary policy is unlikely to pop this bubble. But it could decide
how hard the landing is – and Washington may decide who gets a
parachute.
Bubble or not?
What do valuations and revenue gaps tell
us?
Bubble-like pockets, but not a bubble across the board: the leaders
are hugely proﬁtable, while the real uncertainty sits with the AI

labs and the revenue gap.
Valuations ﬁrst. At its 2000 peak, Cisco traded at well over 100
times earnings. Today’s AI leaders are expensive, but proﬁtable,
and analysts expect earnings growth to bring their multiples down
considerably. Microsoft’s P/E is expected to fall from 29.2x based on
ﬁscal 2026 earnings to 21.9x in 2028, Amazon’s from 28x based on
2025 earnings to 21x in 2027, and Alphabet’s from 20.9x to 17.9x
over the same period. With earnings estimates being higher than a

couple of months ago, that does not look particularly stretched.
The larger uncertainty sits with the AI labs, whose valuations follow
the venture capital playbook: spectacular revenue growth,
uncertain long-term economics.
Then the revenue gap. Capex is running ahead of AI revenues, as it
can take several years for investment to translate fully into
revenues. Amazon, Microsoft and Alphabet have invested around
US$600bn over 2023-25. As a rough rule of thumb, every dollar of

AI infrastructure may need to generate 40-50 cents of annual
revenue (assuming hyperscaler-like margins), implying US$240-
300bn a year – although not all of this capex was AI-related. That
looks large, but less so next to labour costs. At US$100,000 per
employee, US$300bn is the cost of three million workers. AI doesn't

### Page 20

need to replace them: a 20% productivity gain across 15 million
employees would deliver the same value. Consumers alone won't
close the gap; the money has to come from enterprises.
Two caveats. Concentration: the weight of the largest tech stocks in
the S&P 500 exceeds the dotcom peak, so any disappointment
would hit the whole index hard. And circularity in ﬁnancing and
revenues makes true end-demand harder to measure.
Finally, a bubble in prices doesn't mean the technology is a dud.

Railways had a bubble. So did the internet. Both still changed the
world.
What could end the boom prematurely:
disappointing demand, power bottlenecks, a
cheaper-model breakthrough, or Taiwan?
Most likely not a single trigger, but a slow squeeze: overcapacity,
eroding margins and tighter ﬁnancing if the revenue gap fails to
close.
Start with the usual suspects. Demand could disappoint if
enterprise adoption stalls and pilots fail to turn into proﬁts. Power

bottlenecks are more likely to cap the pace of the boom than to end
it, and could even stretch it out. A Taiwan crisis remains a low-
probability, extreme-impact event.
Cheaper models are the most intriguing risk. The DeepSeek shock of
January 2025 wiped almost US$600bn oﬀ Nvidia's market value in
a single day. Cheaper compute can raise demand – the Jevons
paradox – but higher volumes can come with lower margins. That
makes the proﬁtability of AI labs such as OpenAI and Anthropic the

big question mark. If it disappoints, the hardware will still be
needed, but the build-out would likely slow. Nvidia's exceptionally
high margins could also normalise if competition increases or the
scarcity value of leading-edge compute declines.
Missing from most lists is ﬁnancing: rising bond yields, a credit
event at a leveraged neocloud or private credit fund, or an energy
shock that keeps central banks hawkish.

### Page 21

Ultimately, we see the biggest risk in overcapacity. Continued
innovation in chips, more eﬃcient models and optimised
algorithms could eventually create an imbalance between supply
and demand, pushing down utilisation and returns. For now,
though, the industry is still struggling to scale compute and secure
enough power, while new business models and applications keep
emerging.
There is one more way to read today's capacity commitments: as
strategic entry deterrence. Announcing gigantic investment plans

discourages competitors from entering the market – and some of
those plans may never be fully executed if returns disappoint. That
would be a quieter ending than a crash. Not with a bang, but with a
series of downward revisions to capex guidance.
If tech stocks fall 30%, how much does the
wealth effect hit US consumption?
Less than you might think, but more than during the dotcom bust –
and the real damage would come through investment and jobs.
US equity wealth is highly concentrated. The top 20% of

households by income (earning more than $155k a year) hold
84.1% of equities, mutual funds and deﬁned-contribution pension
assets. That limits the wealth eﬀect. Fed research suggests the
marginal propensity to consume out of wealth fell from around 3.5
cents per dollar (1964-2011) to 2.6 cents in 2020.
A 30% stock market correction would wipe out roughly $24.5tn of
household wealth. At 2.6 cents per dollar, that means a drop in
consumer spending of around $640bn, or 2.9% of nominal

consumer spending (an annualised $22.3tn in August). With wealth
even more concentrated than in 2020, the multiplier is probably
weaker; at 2.2 cents, the hit would be around 2.4%. That is a level
eﬀect, typically playing out over one to two years – but a big
number nonetheless.
History oﬀers some comfort: during the dotcom bust, US consumer
spending barely ﬂinched. But it could be worse this time. First,
wealthy households are currently the only ones holding up

### Page 22

spending; the bottom 60%, who spend roughly as many dollars as
the top 20%, are squeezed by tariﬀs and higher fuel prices. Second,
the Fed has less room to cut than in 2001. Third, the credit cushion
is thinner: rising delinquencies on car loans and credit cards show
stress building at lower incomes.
The good news: this is not 2008. US household debt stands at
around 70% of GDP, the same as in 2001 and well below the 100%
reached in 2007.
In the end, the wealth eﬀect is the smaller channel. After the

dotcom bust, business investment fell by 11% in two years. IT
equipment was the least of it, falling just 6%, transport equipment
fell 21% and manufacturing buildings plunged by a whopping
55%. Tech may light the fuse, but the explosion happens elsewhere.
What would a tech sell-off mean for the
dollar and FX markets?
Not necessarily the classic safe-haven dollar rally – this time, a
tech sell-oﬀ could well hurt the dollar.
The usual playbook says risk-oﬀ means dollar up. But an AI-led

correction would be a US-centric shock. Foreign investors hold large,
partly unhedged positions in US equities, built up over a decade of
American outperformance. A sell-oﬀ in US tech could trigger
repatriation and higher hedge ratios, both dollar-negative – as in
the 'sell America' episode of April 2025. The IMF estimates the
dollar would fall by around 6% in an AI-led equity correction.
The rates channel points in the same direction. Negative wealth

eﬀects, weaker consumption and slower US growth would
eventually push the Fed towards rate cuts, narrowing interest rate
diﬀerentials against the dollar. The timing would depend on
inﬂation: with prices still running above target, the Fed may cut
later than markets hope, which could limit the dollar's fall in the
early stages.
Among the winners, the yen and the Swiss franc would play their
traditional safe-haven role. The euro could beneﬁt relatively, given

Europe's lower exposure to both AI capex and equity wealth (4Q). A

### Page 23

tech crash made in America would, for once, be a reason to hold
European assets rather than ﬂee them.
The losers would be the currencies of Asian tech exporters, notably
the Taiwan dollar and the Korean won, whose economies are
leveraged bets on AI demand. They would come under heavy
pressure, although intervention by local authorities and the legacy
of huge trade surpluses would cushion the blow somewhat.
The bottom line: in an AI bust, the dollar may lose its reputation as

the place to hide. Not because the world has found a better safe
haven, but because this time the ﬁre would start in the safe house.
Our base case for 2027: boom, plateau or
bust, and which signposts should readers
watch?
Our base case is a plateau at altitude: the boom continues in 2027,
but its contribution to growth peaks. A bust remains the main risk
scenario.
Early indicators point to tight semiconductor supply into 2028,
suggesting the investment cycle still has legs. But as capex growth

slows, AI's contribution to US growth should fade.
The diﬀerence between the two scenarios matters enormously for
the US. A plateau would give the non-tech economy room to
breathe: capital, energy and workers no longer absorbed by AI
could ﬂow back, allowing non-tech investment – and potentially
employment – to rebound. A bust would be a very diﬀerent animal:
an abrupt choking oﬀ of tech investment, similar to 2001,
combined with a negative wealth eﬀect on consumers through

falling stock markets. And this time, central banks would have less
room for rate cuts as inﬂation remains their main concern.
Which signposts should readers watch? For the boom itself, the key
indicators are hyperscaler capex guidance, Taiwanese order books
and lead times, and AI revenues relative to capex. Markets will focus
on realised AI revenues at the large technology ﬁrms, margins on
incremental revenue and how the next phase of the build-out will

be funded; ﬁgures that disappoint relative to company budgets

On ﬁnancing, credit spreads on AI-related debt, private credit ﬂows

and redemptions, and bond yields and term premia will show
whether investors remain willing to fund the build-out. In the real
economy, the questions are whether non-tech investment ﬁnally
recovers, how hiring in AI-exposed sectors develops, and what
productivity, grid connection and power data are telling us.
The question for 2027 isn't whether AI will change the world. It's
whether anyone gets paid for it in time. If earnings fail to match
sky-high expectations, the plateau could turn out to be a ledge.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets rallied across much of the globe. In the
US, reports of an upgraded outlook for CoreWeave, an
oversubscribed IPO for Anthropic and a stabilisation in
Nvidia supported renewed optimism around AI which

buoyed tech stocks, seeing the S&P 500 (+1.4%) and
Nasdaq (+1.7%) both rally, though the smaller gain for the
Dow Jones (+0.5%) shows the rally was more narrowly
based.
•  Across the pond, the Euro Stoxx 50 continued to build on
recent momentum with another solid gain (+1.0%), while
London’s FTSE 100 bounced back from a modest decline
yesterday (+0.8%). Stocks were up across most of Asia,
including Tokyo (+1.3%), Shanghai (+0.4%) and Seoul
(+2.6%), with only the Hang Seng (–1.0%) bucking the

wider trend. The ASX 200 rose 0.3% yesterday, but weaker
trading in futures overnight points to a mixed open this
morning.
•  Cash trading in bond markets were closed in the US for
Veterans Day, but trading in futures points to a continued
rise in yields following hotter-than-expected inflation
data received earlier in the week, which all but cemented
a December FOMC rate hike. Beyond December, markets
are also pricing in another two rate hikes over the course of

2027.
• Yields were little changed elsewhere, with the 10Y Bund
and Gilt steady on the day. The 10Y JGB meanwhile
continued to sell-off, with yields jumping 10bps to 2.80%
having effectively unwound the rally over late last week.
ACGBs were also weaker during local trading, seeing yields
rise 7bps on the 3Y and 6bps on the 10Y, following the
sharp rise in global yields the day prior post US CPI. Swaps
markets point to no hikes nor cuts from the RBA through to

end-2027 at present.
•  The USD received a solid bid overnight, buoyed by sharp
inflows into AI and tech stocks that improved risk sentiment.
The DXY finished 0.7% higher at around 102.9, the
strongest since April 2025. That move has pushed the Euro
down 0.6% back to a USD1.10 handle, the weakest since
May 2025, while the Yen slipped 0.7% to JPY159.9, the
weakest since the before the early-September rally. While
moves in the AUD was comparatively small overnight,

slipping 0.2% to around the USD0.6850 mark, that move
builds on recent downward momentum and has the currency
delicately positioned near 2026’s lows.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight, stabilising somewhat after
recent declines amid ongoing uncertainty around the future
of the US-Iran War, as President Trump downplays the
possibility of further strikes on Iran all while negotiations
show little progress. Brent rose 1.0% to US$92.4/bbl. The
rebound in risk sentiment buoyed metals, with LME copper

up 1.0% to US$13,817/mt while LME aluminium jumped
1.9% to US$3,068/mt. Gold prices meanwhile dipped 1.2%,
falling back below the US$4,000/oz mark, building on recent
momentum post US CPI which saw real yields jump higher
International Data
There was no major data out yesterday.
Domestic Data
In Australia, the value of dwelling finance approvals surprised
materially to the downside in Q3, the 15.5% decline marking
the largest quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.

The decline looks to be mostly volume-driven, with the number
of loans falling sharply compared to the average size of loans.
Across segments, investor lending values dropped by nearly a
third (see here).

Editorial
Tactical plays within a diversified allocation 
Strategy summary: Our calls to dial down risk since early 
October have helped mitigate a bear market in semiconductors 
and the sharpest single-day post-mid-term election downturn in 
US equities in over three decades. Volatility has eased after the 
post-election spike, helping the IT sector partly recover. We see 
further upside in our opportunistic US IT and healthcare sector 
equity ideas. In bonds, we lock in gains on US inflation-
protected bonds as inflation nears a peak.

mid-cycle market dynamics. Last week, as the US equity 
market volatility index VIX surged above 25, we partially dialled 
up risk with a tactical idea on the more diversified US IT sector. 
Staying tactical within a diversified allocation. Equity 
volatility following last week’s Democratic clean sweep in the 
US elections has subsided. Questions about the pace of AI data 
centre buildout are likely to linger until Democrats take office in 
in January. Nvidia’s turmoil adds uncertainty. We remain 
broadly diversified and balanced in our core allocation across 
assets, sector and regions, while seeking tactical opportunities. 
History suggests sharp selloffs are followed by near-term 
volatility, particularly during macroeconomic stress. However, 
the balance of historical outcomes has typically shifted strongly 
to the upside over a three- to six-month horizon. Besides, 
historically, US equity markets have delivered the strongest 12-
month returns after opposition clean sweeps in the mid-terms. 
Rotation driving US IT sector recovery: As volatility eases, 
our opportunistic US IT equities idea, launched a week ago, has 
performed well, despite some significantly negative company 
specific developments. Attractive valuations support further 
upside. Investors are rotating within IT from semiconductors, 
affected by concerns over the pace of AI data centre buildout 
and Nvidia’s troubles, towards software and hardware 
providers. The latter have strong cash flows and balance sheets 
and are likely to benefit from AI monetisation. Together, they 
account for more than 60% of the IT sector (see page 4).

Remain constructive on US healthcare. US healthcare 
earnings are projected to grow almost 23% in 2027, the second-
fastest among MSCI US sectors. We see scope for further 
upside in the sector as innovation underpins long-term earnings 
growth. With the market rotating away from concentrated AI 
exposure, healthcare's stable, non-cyclical demand profile 
offers a compelling exposure as a diversifier (see page 5). 
Outside the US, Switzerland and India offer diversified 
returns. The Swiss equity market consists of 41% healthcare, 
19% financial services and 14% consumer staples, providing a 
defensive alternative to investors seeking to diversify away 
from tech-heavy exposure. The Indian equity market has been 
classified as an “anti-AI” trade. Historically narrow valuation 
premiums vs. EMs, low foreign investor positioning and c. 15% 
expected 2027 earnings growth make it an attractive diversifier. 
Strong jobs and inflation reports cement December Fed 
rate hike. The US economy added 165,000 jobs in October, 
more than twice the 70,000 consensus estimate. The 
unemployment rate fell to 4.1%. Meanwhile, headline and core 
inflation in October, at 0.4% and 0.3% m/m, respectively, 
exceeded expectations as elevated fuel prices, ‘techflation’ and 
earlier tariffs fed into directly exposed sectors, including food, 
utilities and transportation services. A healthy job market keeps 
the Fed focused on lowering inflation, cementing the prospect 
of a December rate hike, potentially followed by another in Q1 
2027. A strong economy and elevated interest rates support our 
overweight allocation to US financial sector equities. 
Locking in gains from our bullish US inflation-protected 
bonds idea. While near-term price pressures remain elevated, 
we expect inflation to ease with energy costs over the coming 
year. Republicans’ heavy losses in the US mid-term elections 
increase pressure on the Trump administration to end the Iran 
conflict. One-year US inflation expectations have slumped 
alongside oil prices. A potential slowdown in the pace of AI 
buildout is also likely to ease techflation. Meanwhile, Fed rate 
hikes should further tighten financial conditions, tempering 
price pressures. This disinflationary outlook makes the risk-
reward for US inflation-protected bonds less compelling. We 
close the idea, locking in a 4.4% gain (page 6). 
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

What does history tell us about the aftermath of a single-
day sell-off of 4% or more in the S&P500 index?

Our view: The S&P500 fell 4.36% on 5 November 2026, a rare daily 
move of 3.8 standard deviations. Since 2009, there have been only

24 occasions when the index declined by 4% or more in a single

session. The immediate aftermath of such sell-offs has historically

been mixed. One-month returns ranged from around -15% to

+23%, with positive returns in 70% of cases. The weaker

outcomes were concentrated around periods of significant macro

stress, including the tail end of the 2008 Global Financial Crisis

(GFC), the 2020 COVID-19 pandemic and the 2022 monetary policy

tightening cycle. 
The historical picture becomes considerably more constructive

beyond the first month. All 24 episodes generated positive three-
month returns, ranging from roughly +0.5% to +38%, while six-
month returns were positive in 96% of cases, with gains reaching 
as much as +49%. Overall, history suggests sharp sell-offs can be 
followed by near-term volatility, particularly during periods of broader 
macro stress. However, the balance of historical outcomes has 
shifted strongly to the upside over a 3-6-month horizon. 
—  Maggie Au Yeung, Quantitative Analyst 
 o
— Francis Lim, Senior Quantitative Strategist
Where do you see the most favourable risk-reward within

the US technology sector? 
Our view: We favour high-quality Big Tech companies with 
strong balance sheets and prefer diversified exposure through 
our US Information Technology (IT) opportunistic idea. 
Rationale: The recent pullback in US semiconductor stocks has 
improved their risk-reward profile. Earnings for these companies are 
forecast to grow by 65% in 2027, the fastest rate among technology 
subsectors. Despite this outlook, recent regulatory and corporate 
governance concerns are weighing on sentiment. Potential state-
level restrictions on data centre development following the recent 
US mid-term election results could weigh on semiconductor demand 
growth. However, we believe these risks are manageable and 
continued AI adoption should support demand for AI inference. We 
prefer diversified exposure to quality semiconductor 
companies with durable demand and strong volume growth. 
To navigate volatility in the US technology sector, we favour Big Tech 
companies with strong balance sheets and resilient cash generation, 
as they are better positioned to sustain AI investments without 
relying heavily on external financing. Our US IT opportunistic idea 
combines these preferences through exposure to semiconductors, 
software and hardware, allowing investors to participate in a 
potential recovery across a broader range of earnings drivers.
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d)

Do fundamental drivers support a constructive view on the 
US healthcare sector?

Our view: We remain constructive on US healthcare, supported

by its defensive characteristics and robust earnings growth.

Rationale: US healthcare earnings are projected to grow by 22.5% 
in 2027, the second-fastest among MSCI US sectors, driven by 
improving biopharma earnings. The US healthcare sector's 
valuation gap against the broader market has been recovering from 
a steep historical discount a year ago. The sector's forward price-to-

earnings (P/E) ratio of c. 20x now exceeds the broader US market’s

c. 18.4x, but we see scope for the valuation to be supported amid

investor demand for defensive growth. The sector’s broad legislative

policy overhang has materially faded following the US election cycle,

while targeted regulatory and oversight risks remain on the watchlist.

Healthcare innovation also underpins structural growth. Wider

adoption of obesity and diabetes treatments, alongside advances in

oncology treatments, medical devices and robotic surgery, should

support long-term earnings growth. With the market rotating away

from concentrated AI exposure, healthcare's stable, non-cyclical 
demand profile presents compelling opportunities for investors 
seeking both defensiveness and growth in the near term.
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst
What is your outlook for China’s Big Tech sector? 
Our view: We remain positive on China’s Big Tech sector, 
consistent with our Overweight stance on the country’s IT and 
communication services sector. 
Rationale: Valuations across China’s technology sector remain 
inexpensive at 14.2x, with investor attention centred on whether AI-
driven revenue can offset elevated AI-related spending, which is 
currently compressing margins. Elevated AI capex and negative 
free cash flow remain key investor concerns in the Q3 earnings 
season. Top-line momentum also remains uneven: soft domestic 
consumption continues to weigh on e-commerce, advertising and 
local services, whereas gaming and cloud computing are 
delivering resilient growth. The broader narrative is steadily 
transitioning towards commercialisation, with rapid enterprise 
penetration by AI workplace agents and upcoming AI model 
launches shifting the market focus towards active deployment and 
conversion to paid usage. Evolving regulatory frameworks 
covering AI governance, agent self-regulation, tax scrutiny and 
compliance requirements remain important variables influencing 
medium-term margin trajectories. Ultimately, valuation support, 
gradual monetisation of AI capabilities and resilient core 
segments support our Overweight positioning.
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

grow 22.5% in 2027 – the second-fastest among

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

Why are you closing your US Treasury Inflation-Protected

Securities (TIPS) opportunistic idea? 
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish US TIPS 
position, as the risk-reward profile has become less compelling.

The trade returned 4.4% since its initiation on 11 April 2025.

Rationale: We initiated an opportunistic bullish position in US TIPS

on 11 April 2025 as an oil and inflation hedge, expecting geopolitical

uncertainty to keep inflation expectations elevated. The risk-reward,

however, has since become less compelling, as hawkish Fed 
commentary, easing geopolitical risks and a potential slowdown in 
the pace of AI buildout are now likely to ease broader inflation and 
‘techflation’ pressures. We have therefore closed the opportunistic

idea, realising a gain of 4.4%.
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist
How do falling oil prices and October’s US inflation data 
affect your Fed and bond market outlook? 
Our view: Our base case envisages two further 25bps Fed hikes 
by mid-2027, with scope for cuts in H2 2027 if energy prices 
remain consistently lower and growth trajectory normalises. We 
favour income over chasing duration gains, focusing on higher-
quality corporate bonds with 3-7-year maturities. 
Rationale: Lower oil prices have a two-sided effect: they ease 
inflationary pressures and support growth by boosting purchasing 
power and lowering business costs. Cheaper energy, therefore, 
does not automatically justify Fed easing. Our base case envisages 
two further 25bps hikes by mid-2027, with scope for cuts in H2 
2027 if energy prices remain consistently lower. Sustained 
moderation in core inflation would challenge the near-term tightening 
path. However, October’s US consumer inflation print reinforced our 
assessment that each Fed meeting now remains a ‘live’ decision. 
With the US 10-year government bond yield already within our 5.0-
5.25% 3-month range, we favour income over chasing duration 
gains. Credit performance has diverged: Developed Market high-
yield bonds have outperformed both investment-grade credit and 
government bonds. Credit still offers attractive income, and we retain 
our preference for 3-7-year maturities, although heavy primary 
issuance remains a counterweight. We favour selective carry, with a 
preference for higher-quality corporate bonds. These include bonds 
issued by leading AI hyperscalers, where risk-reward has become 
more attractive. Our emphasis is on generating steady income, 
rather than relying on further declines in bond yields or spreads.
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist
While real (net-of-inflation) yields remain elevated, 
the inflation-protection function of US TIPS has 
diminished as inflation expectations ease

Top client questions (cont’d)
Does the USD’s rally have further to run?

Our view: Further upside appears limited. Stronger-than-

expected US core inflation in October and hawkish Fed

comments have pushed the US Dollar Index (DXY) above 103.

However, markets already price in more Fed tightening than we

expect. We see the DXY returning to its 101.00-102.95 range in

the near term, before gradually weakening towards our 12-

month forecast of 98.5.

Rationale: US core consumer prices rose 0.3% m/m in October,

above the 0.2% m/m expected, meeting Fed Governor Waller’s

threshold for considering a 50bps December rate hike. This pushed

US government bond yields higher, supporting the USD rally. 
However, futures now imply a Fed rate peak of 4.50-4.75% by

mid-2027, compared with our expectation of 4.25-4.50% following

two 25bps hikes, one in December and the second in H1 2027. We

expect the Fed to begin easing in H2 2027. As market expectations 
gradually converge towards our view, USD support from US bond 
yields should weaken.
Meanwhile, falling oil and petrol prices should help ease headline 
inflation in the coming months, reducing pressure for further Fed 
tightening. This reinforces our expectation of limited USD upside, 
although persistent services inflation remains a key risk.
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist
Do you expect USD/JPY to sustainably break out above 
160 in the near term?
Our view: We see USD/JPY rangebound with a downside bias, 
as growing BoJ tightening expectations and potential policy 
convergence will support gradual JPY appreciation.
Rationale: Renewed tech optimism has encouraged the re-
establishment of carry trades, providing support to the pair lately. 
Meanwhile, US core inflation beat expectations for a third 
consecutive month in October, reinforcing expectations for a 
December Fed hike. Further upside inflation surprises could widen 
the US-Japan interest rate differential and push USD/JPY higher. 
However, the risk of intervention by Japan’s Ministry of Finance 
is likely to rise materially around 160, limiting the scope for 
sustained upside. On the downside, a 25bps BoJ hike to 1.50%, 
accompanied by clear signals of consecutive hikes in March and 
June 2027, would be a significant yen-positive catalyst. A more non-
linear downside scenario would be a disorderly equity sell-off, which 
could trigger rapid JPY appreciation through carry-trade unwinding. 
We see scope for the pair to test support towards 156 in the 
coming weeks.
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist
US dollar index’s rally above 103 looks stretched

KEY DATA RELEASES AND EVENTS NEXT WEEK 
• China’s exports growth should ease on slowing momentum 
• India’s CPI could bump up on higher food costs 
• Taiwan’s exports could moderate as the AI cycle normalizes 
The Week Ahead covers the 
key data releases and central 
bank events of the coming 
week, collating our macro 
forecasts.

The Week Ahead 
Forecasts, data preview, central bank 
watch

6 November 2026

Taimur Baig PhD, Chief

### Page 2

The Week Ahead November 6, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Taiwan

Export growth is expected to ease to 
44.4% yoy in October from 60.9% in 
September. A higher comparison base 
makes exceptional export growth 
difficult to sustain from 4Q onwards. The 
global AI cycle is also expected to settle 
into a more normal pace into 2027, as 
higher interest rates lift capital costs 
and US mid-term election results 
heighten regulatory uncertainty. These 
factors underpin our forecast for 
Taiwan’s GDP growth to return to high 
single digits from 4Q onwards and into 
2027.

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s industrial 
production growth to rise to 5.5% yoy in 
September 2026 from 5.0% yoy in 
August. The uptick was likely driven by 
export-oriented manufacturing as 
strong artificial intelligence (AI)-related 
demand continued to support growth in 
electrical and electronics goods, 
alongside a smaller contraction in mining.

China

Export growth is expected to decelerate 
from 36.3% yoy in September to 28.1% in 
October. Both the official manufacturing 
PMI and new export orders sub-index fell 
below 50 after two months in expansion 
territory. High-frequency trade indicators 
also point to slowing momentum, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage handled 
at China’s 20 major ports falling from 7.2% 
yoy in September to -4.2% in October. 
Freight activity weakened as well, with 
growth in international cargo flights 
dropping from 3.6% yoy to -5.9% over the 
same period. On the price front, factory-gate 
and raw material price pressures moderated, 
with the main raw material purchasing price 
and producer price sub-indices declining 
from 60.8 and 54.0 to 56.8 and 51.7, 
respectively. Despite remaining above the 
50 threshold, the moderation suggests some 
easing in pipeline price pressures. 
Downstream consumer price pressures are 
also likely to remain subdued amid soft 
domestic demand. As a result, CPI inflation 
is expected to ease slightly from 0.9% yoy in 
September to 0.8% in October.

India

We expect headline inflation to rise to 6.0% 
yoy in October from 5.5% the month before, 
on higher food costs and a low base effect. 
High frequency data pointed to a further 
pick-up in sequential pressures in food 
segments, across cereals, edible oil, 
perishables (vegetables) etc. Non-food 
components should find relief from further 
moderation in precious metals and delay in 
passage of retail fuel price changes. Core 
inflation is nonetheless set to quicken to 4.5-
4.6% as services notch increases and as 
indirect tax effects in the base, fall out of the 
equation. With inflation expected to test 
past the higher end of the RBI target range, 
we expect a second successive rate hike in 
December. The decline in oil prices has also 
led to cooling of overnight OIS rates as 
concerns over aggressive rate hikes have 
eased. Goods trade deficit is likely to stay 
wide at above $30bn in Oct.

Document 419: Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade

Source PDF: all report pdf/Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Global trade will remain resilient in 2027, but the focus will
shift from tariﬀs to ownership, technology and investment.
While not our base case, an escalation in an EU-China trade
war would rank among the most signiﬁcant risks to global
trade

Inga Fechner and Carsten Brzeski
Europe is undoubtedly facing a second 'China shock' – only this time around,
it's largely negative
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Trade survives politics
Your privacy is important

### Page 2

Global goods trade continues to weather geopolitical shocks.
Over the past few years, trade has proved far more resilient
than global headlines suggest. We expect that resilience to
continue into 2027. Disruptions in global shipping, shifting
tariﬀ regimes and recurring geopolitical tensions have
repeatedly altered trade routes without derailing trade itself.
Strong demand for AI-related investment and infrastructure
should continue to support trade ﬂows, particularly across

Asia, while selective US-China decoupling is likely to beneﬁt
third countries that attract production and investment. For
2027, we expect global goods trade to remain positive,
expanding about 3% year-on-year. In short, trade survives
politics in 2027 because companies can still adapt faster
than governments can restrict them.
Our risky call: Ownership matters more than
trade
Tariﬀs were only the ﬁrst step. By 2027, the real battleground shifts
to capital, technology and ownership. As companies invest behind

tariﬀ walls, governments stop merely screening new investment
and begin requiring companies to restructure, separate sensitive
operations or divest existing strategic assets.
A foreign-owned battery plant, semiconductor facility, or data
centre once supported for bringing local investment and jobs is
now treated as a national-security risk. Companies that once
navigated geopolitical tensions through diversiﬁcation ﬁnd
themselves forced to choose sides. The question is no longer who

exports the most, but who controls the productive capacity on
which economies depend. Trade would continue, but the corporate
structures that have kept it moving would start to fracture as
governments alter trade ﬂows. This could potentially cause global
goods trade growth to stall or even contract in 2027.
Our bold call: EU-China trade war

### Page 3

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes
irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment
objectives. The information does not constitute investment recommendation,
and nor is it investment, legal or tax advice or an oﬀer or solicitation to
purchase or sell any ﬁnancial instrument.
Read more
Download
Included in the following bundle
The EU and China enter an outright trade conﬂict. Europe abandons

its largely open-market approach and increasingly applies
reciprocity principles to market access and investment. Chinese
ﬁrms investing in strategic sectors face mandatory 50:50 joint
ventures, local ownership requirements and growing restrictions on
acquisitions.
At the same time, the EU imposes broad tariﬀs on Chinese imports,
with rates reaching 50% in politically sensitive sectors such as
vehicles, chemicals, machinery and electronics. China retaliates

with measures of similar scale.
The consequences are signiﬁcant. China accounts for roughly 15%
of global goods trade, while the EU accounts for around 13.5%.
Together they represent almost 30% of world goods trade. With the
EU being China's largest trading partner in goods and China being
the EU's second-largest trading partner in goods, a full-scale trade
conﬂict would rank among the most consequential economic
conﬂicts of the modern era.

Document 427: US MARKETS CALL_ S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar
Date:    5 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500
Valuation Dives As Earnings
Soar
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Baptiste Azais / Unsplash
The stock market dived last week. Investors priced in political backlash
against data centers after Democrats swept Congress. We anticipated
buying opportunities after the midterm elections, and Thursday’s selloff
delivered some. This remains an earnings-led bull market. Valuations are
falling while earnings are soaring. We still view the AI backlash as another

### Page 3

speed bump, not a roadblock. We are sticking with our 7,900 year-end
target for the S&P 500.
I. Performance
The Nasdaq entered correction territory on Thursday and closed the week
below its 200-day moving average (chart). It has done this before recently.
It rebounded sharply after its Spring 2025 "Liberation Day" plunge and
again after the Iran war started earlier this year, quickly resuming its
uptrend both times.
The equal-weight S&P 500 is holding up remarkably well. It remains near

its recent record high and above both its 50-day and 200-day moving
averages (chart). The damage has been concentrated in the AI trade, not
the broader market.

### Page 4

The Magnificent-7 are underperforming again. The MAGS ETF is up only
2.4% ytd, versus 7.8% for the S&P 500 (chart). The Impressive-493 also
took a hit this week. The XMAGS ETF includes Micron and the other
semiconductor stocks hit hardest in the selloff. It is still up 10.0% ytd.
Dow Theory remains bullish. The Dow Jones Industrial Average is holding
up well near its highs (chart). The Transports have lagged. They should
improve if oil prices continue to fall.

II. Earnings
Earnings season picked up this week. The blended Q3 S&P 500 EPS
growth estimate (i.e., actual results for companies that have reported,
estimates for those that haven’t yet) rose to 25.9% y/y from 23.8% a week
earlier, helped by AMD’s solid results. Q4’s estimate edged down to 29.7%

### Page 5

(chart). As we noted previously, Q2’s 50.4% y/y EPS growth rate was
boosted by mark-to-market gains at Alphabet and Amazon. Excluding
those, growth was closer to 25%, which is still very strong.
Forward earnings rose to a record $418.25. It is converging on the 2027
estimate of $426.28, which continues to climb (chart). Analysts’ 2028
consensus S&P 500 EPS estimate rose to $495.14; it’s been closing in on
$500—which is our target for 2030!  We are bullish, but not as bullish as

the analysts. The drop in the forward P/E this year suggests that investors
also believe analysts are too bullish on earnings.
Forward earnings for the S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 all rose to
record highs again last week (chart). Earnings strength remains broad.

### Page 6

S&P 500 forward revenues per share is up 15.9% y/y, while forward
earnings per share is up 39.0% y/y (chart).
III. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E fell to 17.6 last week, below the 2025 Liberation
Day low (chart). P/E plunges typically accompany corrections and
recessions. The economy shows no sign of a recession anytime soon.
Investors instead fear irrational exuberance in earnings forecasts.

### Page 7

Since the bull market began on October 12, 2022, the S&P 500 has risen
106.2%. Forward earnings is up 75.5%, while the forward P/E is up 22.5%
(chart). The P/E and E both drove the rally until the forward P/E peaked in
October last year. Since then, E has done all the work. Investors are
finding it harder to predict the return on all the AI capital spending. They
won’t pay an excessive multiple given that uncertainty.
The S&P 500 Semiconductors industry accounts for much of the P/E drop.

The industry index's forward P/E has plunged to 13.1, well below the S&P
500’s (chart). Semis are now cheaper than they were during the 2022
bear market.

### Page 8

IV. Fixed Income
The futures market expects about two rate hikes over the next 12 months.
That explains why the 2-year Treasury yield remains well above the 3.88%
federal funds rate, even after easing to 4.46% following the midterms
(chart).
The 10-year Treasury yield continues to fall. It dropped to 4.84% and is
testing its uptrend line from last year’s low (chart). We’ll see if it bounces.
Either way, it is back in the 4.00%-5.00% “old normal” range where we feel

comfortable.

### Page 9

The 10-year TIPS yield fell to 2.62%. The yield spread between the 10-
year Treasury and TIPS, a widely used proxy for expected inflation, eased
to 2.22% (chart). That proxy tends to move with oil prices. The recent drop
in crude showed up here.
V. Commodities
The price of Brent crude oil fell to $98.32 a barrel in recent days (chart).
No news of the re-escalation of the war in the Middle East has emerged
since the midterms. Hopes for a US-Iran deal are reflected in the oil

market. We still see a risk of re-escalation.

### Page 10

Nearby gasoline futures dropped to $2.78 a gallon last week (chart). If
sustained, that should start to show up as lower inflation in a couple of
months. Renewed fighting in the Middle East would reverse that.
Steel prices show no sign of a slowdown in data center construction. US
Midwest hot-rolled coil rose to $1,357 a ton, the highest since 2022
(chart).

### Page 11

Agricultural prices have eased. The S&P GSCI Agriculture Spot index fell
to 376.77 last week (chart). Fears of a food price spike should subside.

◼ 金融市場：短線股市反應可能大於政策本身的
實質影響，尤其是AI相關供應鏈或科技權重占
比高的市場。在政策不確定消除與籌碼沉澱前，
策略上維持多元配置，並建議保留部位彈性。
◼ 投資建議：美國期中選舉結果引發AI政策疑慮，
在金融市場劇烈波動之際，投資人應重新檢視
風險胃納與配置初衷，以股債配置為主軸，並
運用多元資產工具與結構型產品，來追求長期
穩定回報。
熱門議題/資產影響推估 市場焦點
➢ 美國政策風險增加：民主黨全面掌控國會後，市
場預期大型科技與AI產業未來可能面臨更嚴格監
管，加上投資人對AI高額資本支出與獲利前景轉
趨保守，引發科技與相關區域市場大幅回調。
➢ 市場焦點放在AI資本支出與獲利成長：本行認為，
資料中心建設將面臨更嚴格的監管、較長的審批
流程、較少的稅收優惠，但並不表示建設將會停
止；且預估對於輝達與超微等指標半導體公司
2028年的獲利影響僅2-5%。

### Page 3

美國國會變天， AI監管預期拖累科技股、殖利率回落
AI概念受挫，資金輪動至其他板塊
盤勢回顧
美10年期利率回到5%以下，信用利差自高點收斂
3
• AI概念股領跌，拖累全球科技股走弱：昨日(11/5)美股重挫主要受民主黨全面掌控國會後，市場預期大型科技
與AI產業未來可能面臨更嚴格監管，加上投資人對AI高額資本支出與獲利前景轉趨保守，引發科技股大幅回調。
使AI、半導體及雲端服務類股成為跌勢重心，並拖累日、台、韓科技權重市場同步走弱。不過資金並未全面撤
離股市，而是輪動至金融、醫療保健及防禦型產業，支撐美股等權重整體指數表現相對穩定。
• 財政紛擾仍存但支出展望中性，配合地緣趨緩預期，美10年債利率來到5%以下：同時，民主黨勝選後財政擴
張預期降溫，再加上地緣政治有望緩和，布蘭特原油跌破100美元/桶，支持美國公債利率持續走低至5%以下，
自10月高點以來下滑近50個基點。殖利率下滑輔以金融市場不確定因素降低支撐股市反彈，同步帶動美國信用
債利差收斂，11月以來美國信用債總報酬緩步走揚。

資料來源：左， Bloomberg ，2026/11/，右，Bloomberg，2026/11/6。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/5

### Page 4

©  CTBC
美對資料中心監管程度將提高，但非禁止建置
對資料中心監管程度將提高，但非禁止建置
事件說明
政策目標是要求資料中心將公共成本內部化
4
• 部分當選議員對AI發展提出較極端言論，引起市場擔憂：美國期中選舉結果底定後，部分國會議員隨即發表未
來政策看法，密西根州參議員Abdul El-Sayed表示應該立即停止資料中心的建置，亞利桑那州眾議院
Yassamin Ansari表示應該將暫停AI發展列為新會期的優先事項。這些發言顯示，部分議員的訴求已經超出個
別資料中心開發案，延伸至更廣泛的AI發展限制，引起市場擔憂。
• 對資料中心監管程度將提高，但非禁止建置：本行認為，資料中心建設將面臨更嚴格的監管、較長的審批流程、
較少的稅收優惠，但並不表示建設將會停止。主因包含：(1)為回應生活成本上升的民意，需要讓企業承受更高
的成本，如目前正在推動的ratepayer protection act要求資料中心開發商必需負擔電力的增量成本，但尖端科
技發展仍是跨黨派共識；(2)各州長的立場多為暫停建設，直到新規範制定完成，而非廢止建設；(3)在聯邦層

級，大規模的限制措施即使被國會提出，也很可能被川普否決；(4)企業可前往對資料中心開發較友善的州，如
路易斯安那、北達可他州，甚至轉向海外，不過移向海外應也非民主黨所樂見。
資料來源：左， 高盛，2026/11，右，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11

停建政策有利改善雲運算巨頭 2028 自由現金流 2028年半導體多個產業獲利動能將趨緩
雲運算巨頭可望自停建得利，半導體遭遇美國政策風險
資料來源：左，Bloomberg、中國信託商業銀行投顧中心預期；右，Bloomberg。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/06
• 雲運算巨頭中期將受惠於資料中心限建令：藍色浪潮讓資料中心建設步調放緩勢在必行。雲運算巨頭選前因不
能落後的理由導致競逐資本支出，但選後在政策大旗下將有理由放緩投資節奏。考量強勁算力需求與強勁核心業
務，本行認為放緩投資節奏不致對雲運算巨頭的營運面有過大負面影響。但將有利雲運算巨頭 2028 年的自由現
金流、借貸成本、評價及對晶片、硬體廠商與大語言模型的議價力。本行看好雲運算巨頭是此波修正下的受益者。
• 美國政策風險增加，半導體2028年或將遭遇逆風：新任民主黨德州州長若祭出暫停令或更嚴格審查，將影響美
國資料中心3~6 GW興建。本行預估對於輝達與超微等指標半導體公司2028年的獲利影響僅2-5%。另外，選後

(11/6)谷哥則宣布將投資170億美元在美國懷俄明州(新任州長為共和黨)興建該州第一座大型AI資料中心，顯示雲
端業者正將資料中心建設轉到其他「共和黨執政州」。本行認為2027年半導體產業獲利成長仍將超過五成，但
2028年受資本支出趨緩影響，則會進一步減速。考量費半指數預估本益比目前僅較3月美伊衝突和2025/4美國關
稅高12-14%，顯示市場已反映2028調降風險，後續評價面壓縮空間有限。

台灣重量級科技股獲利預估及評價 AI評價修正 VS 川普關稅震撼(融資減少幅度)
AI成長趨勢未變，關注逢低布局時間點
資料來源：左，彭博，2026/11/6，右，證交所，2026/11/5。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/6
• AI監管疑慮引發科技股估值修正：美國期中選舉民主黨掌控國會，AI監管疑慮引發科技股尤其是AI股的估值大幅
修正，然而，政策風險不等於AI需求反轉，目前市場仍預估2028年CSP資本支出高於2027年。根據TrendForce
的預估，2027年AI伺服器出貨年增36%，GPU整櫃與自研ASIC仍將同步擴張，支撐台灣晶圓代工、先進封裝、
伺服器組裝、散熱與電源等供應鏈的營運成長。後續雖須持續追蹤監管及CAPEX調整等風險，但目前台灣AI供應
鏈整體營運展望正向，建議趁估值回檔之際，逢低挑選具技術優勢、訂單能見度及獲利成長的優質標的。
• AI資本支出被迫延緩疑慮，帶動AI評價下修：隨台股指數在2026年10月創下49969點(逼近5萬點整數關卡)後，

行情急轉直下，截至11/5指數最低來到41514點。如果從10/21起跌點統計，跌幅約達12.1%，融資餘額減肥的
幅度約達10.5%，融資餘額降至5764億，主要原因是民主黨將對未來的AI資本支出控管，引發市場擔憂相關族群
的獲利成長性。對比2026年7月AI高估值修正，指數跌幅15.8%，融資降幅19.8%相比，預期11/6指數大跌，融
資餘額將進一步清洗，整波融資降幅有機會接近2026年7月那波降幅，籌碼料將初步獲得穩定。

美股隱含波動度期限結構轉為倒掛，波動風險攀升
資料來源：(左) Bloomberg；(右) OFR，2026/11/5。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/6
信用與流動性僅小幅轉弱顯示風險未全面擴散
9
市場觀點
• 持倉指標下降疊加避險情緒上升，短線仍須留意股市波動：近期受到AI投資報酬疑慮的影響，對沖基金（以動
能與波動度進行操作之策略）減碼使得美股持倉指標由過熱水位轉折向下；加上期中選舉後市場對AI政策不確定
性的擔憂放大，帶動美股短端波動度快速攀升，美股隱含波動度期限結構轉為倒掛，意味市場避險情緒攀升之下，
股市仍可能出現拋售壓力；此外，指數期貨價格買賣價差擴大下，股市價格波動將加劇，股市流動性轉弱，短線
仍須留意市場波動。
• 信用與流動性仍穩健，研判後續出現大幅回檔的機率不高：從其他構面來看，信用利差雖然攀升但幅度不大，
信用違約交換相對信用利差並未出現大幅拉升的跡象，顯示本次波動未擴及信用風險，主要是避險情緒所致，預

期後續信用利差不致大幅擴張。流動性來看，美債、美元流動性並未出現大幅惡化的現象，顯示整體市場流動性
狀況仍穩健。綜合多個指標的金融壓力指數也僅小幅攀升，在股市波動未進一步擴散的狀況下，擴展到其他資產
的機率較低。

• 政策不確定消除與籌碼沉澱前，宜保留部位彈性：美國AI政策雜音雖提高市場不確定性，但考量過去一年市場

其是AI相關供應鏈或科技權重占比高的市場。在政策不確定消除與籌碼沉澱前，策略上維持多元配置，並建議保

限，並支撐整體指數表現，很大一部分來自市場對民主黨執政下的中東局勢降溫抱有期待。須留意的是，即便國
會由民主黨掌控，但川普對軍事、外交仍有掌控權，且距離新國會上任仍有兩個月的時間，若中東局勢再次惡化，
加上CSP業者對未來資本支出做出調整應對，股市不排除再往下測底部。

Document 556: 価格転嫁の「第二波」に警戒（26年10月企業物価指数） ～川下への転嫁が続くなか、川上価格が再上昇。物価高長期化のリスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/価格転嫁の「第二波」に警戒（26年10月企業物価指数） ～川下への転嫁が続くなか、川上価格が再上昇。物価高長期化のリスク～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年11 月 12 日

1/4 
価格転嫁の「第二波」に警戒（26 年 10 月企業物価指数） 
～川下への転嫁が続くなか、川上価格が再上昇。物価高長期化のリスク～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 10 月の国内企業物価指数は前月比＋0.7％、前年比＋7.1％。前年比では２ヵ月ぶりに＋７％
台へ再加速し、市場予想を大幅に上回る強い結果。農林水産物価格が大幅に下落する一方、
石油・化学関連を中心に幅広い品目で価格が上昇した。 
○ 石油化学関連では、川中の合成樹脂価格が下落し、川下のプラスチック製品では値上がりが
続くという従来の構図に大きな変化はない。一方、川上では資源価格の再上昇を背景に価格
が大きく上昇し、新たなコスト上昇圧力が生じている。 
○ 既往のコスト高の川下への転嫁が続くなか、資源高に伴う川上価格再上昇の影響が今後川中・
川下にも波及する可能性がある。価格転嫁の「第二波」が生じることで、企業物価の上昇圧
力が長期化するリスクが高まっている。 
○ 米価格の大幅下落が続く一方、飲食料品の価格上昇率は高まっており、食料品へのコスト転
嫁圧力も強い。企業段階での物価上昇圧力が消費者物価にも波及することで、27 年初には消
費者物価上昇率が前年比＋３％を上回ると予想する。

1. 事前予想を大幅に上回り、前年比は＋７％台へ再加速 
日本銀行から公表された 2026 年 10 月の国内企業物価指数は、前月比＋0.7％、前年比＋7.1％
となった。前月比では３ヵ月ぶりの上昇であり、伸び率も非常に高い。前年比でも９月の＋6.8％
から伸びを高め、２ヵ月ぶりに＋７％台となっている。市場予想の前年比＋6.5％を大きく上回っ
ており、レンジ（同＋6.1％～＋6.8％）上限も突き抜けた。企業段階での物価上昇圧力が再び強
まっていることが確認される結果である。 
今回注目されるのは、資源価格が上向いたことを背景に、石油・化学関連価格が再び上昇に転
じたことである。９月までは石油・石炭製品や合成樹脂などの価格上昇が一服する一方、プラス
チック製品など川下では値上げが続くという構図となっていた。それに対して 10 月は、川下への
既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が続くなか、川上からの上昇圧力が再び強まった点が特徴である。 
前月比の内訳をみると、石油・石炭製品が同＋2.9％（寄与度：＋0.20％Pt）、化学製品が同＋

### Page 2

2/4 
1.9％（同＋0.15％Pt）と大きく上昇した。このほか、電力・都市ガス・水道が前月比＋2.4％
（寄与度：＋0.15％Pt）、鉄鋼が同＋2.0％（同＋0.11％Pt）、飲食料品が同＋0.6％（同＋0.08％
Pt）、プラスチック製品が同＋1.6％（同＋0.06％Pt）となるなど、幅広い品目で価格上昇がみら
れた。なお、夏季電力料金の影響を除いた国内企業物価指数は前月比＋0.9％であり、この影響を
除けば、10 月の伸びはさらに大きかったことになる。 
一方、農林水産物は前月比▲6.6％（寄与度：▲0.28％Pt）と、９月に続いて大幅に下落した。
前年比でも▲25.0％と大幅なマイナスとなっている。玄米（前月比▲19.0％）、精米（同
▲10.1％）の下落が目立つ。2026 年産米への切り替わりが進むなか、米価の下落が押し下げ要因
となっている。こうした農林水産物の大幅下落にもかかわらず、国内企業物価が前月比＋0.7％
（夏季電力料金調整後：同＋0.9％）と高い伸びになったことは、企業段階での物価上昇圧力の強
さを示している。

2. 川上価格が再び上昇、価格転嫁の「第二波」の可能性も 
石油化学関連では、川上・川中・川下の価格動向に９月までとは異なる動きがみられた。これ
までは、川上・川中の価格にやや落ち着きがみられる一方、川下では春先の原材料高を受けた価
格転嫁が続くという構図だった。10 月も川中の合成樹脂価格は下落し、川下のプラスチック製品
では値上がりが続くなど、川中・川下の動きは概ね変わらない。しかし、川上では資源価格の再
上昇を背景に石油化学関連価格が再び大きく上昇しており、新たなコスト上昇圧力が生じている
点が大きな違いである。 
川上の動きを詳しくみると、石油・石炭製品は前月比＋2.9％と、４ヵ月ぶりに上昇した。この
うちナフサは同＋5.6％となった。また、化学製品に含まれる石油化学系基礎製品も同＋9.1％と
大きく上昇した。内訳では、エチレンが同＋6.6％、プロピレンが同＋6.6％、ベンゼンが同＋
（出所）日本銀行「企業物価指数」

3/4 
7.6％、キシレンが同＋17.1％となるなど、幅広い品目で値上がりがみられた。資源価格の再上昇
が影響したとみられる。 
一方、川中に位置する合成樹脂は前月比▲0.6％と、３ヵ月連続で下落した。ポリエチレンが同
▲1.6％、ポリプロピレンが同▲1.5％となるなど、主要品目でも下落が続いている。川上価格が
再び上昇に転じるなかでも、川中ではこれまでの原材料価格下落の影響が依然残っているとみら
れる。 
これに対して、川下の価格上昇は続いている。プラスチック製品は 10 月に前月比＋1.6％と８
カ月連続で上昇し、前年比でも＋14.1％と９月の＋12.3％から一段と伸びを高めた。品目別で
は、輸送機械用プラスチック製品が前月比＋4.8％、電気機器用プラスチック製品が同＋5.1％と
なったほか、再生プラスチック成形材料も同＋20.7％と大きく上昇した。９月はプラスチック製
容器など包装資材の値上がりが目立ったが、10 月は自動車や電気機器向けの部材でも価格上昇が
進んでいる点が目につく。 
今回重要なのは、既往のコスト高の川下への転嫁が続くなか、新たな価格上昇圧力が川上に生
じていることである。川中・川下ではこれまでの価格動向が概ね続いている一方、川上では資源
価格の再上昇が新たなコスト増につながり始めた。今後、この川上価格の上昇が時間差を伴って
川中・川下へ波及すれば、既往のコスト高の転嫁に新たな値上げが重なることになる。価格転嫁
の「第二波」が生じる可能性があり、物価上昇圧力が長期化することが懸念される。

3. 物価上昇圧力が長期化する可能性 
先行きの企業物価も、高い伸びが続く可能性が高い。10 月の輸入物価指数は円ベースで前月比
＋3.3％と、９月の同▲0.7％から上昇に転じた。契約通貨ベースでも同＋2.3％と上昇している。
なかでも石油・石炭・天然ガスは契約通貨ベースで同＋5.3％、円ベースで同＋6.4％と大きく上
昇した。 
こうした輸入物価上昇は、今後、時間差を伴って国内の川中・川下価格にも波及するとみられ
る。川下では既往のコスト高の価格転嫁がなお続いているほか、物流費や人件費などの上昇も企
業の価格設定に影響している。こうしたなか、新たに資源高によるコスト上昇が加わることで、
物価上昇圧力が長期化する可能性がある。 
こうしたことから、国内企業物価の前年比上昇率は当面＋７％前後の高い伸びが続く可能性が
高い。コメ価格の下落は引き続き下押しとなるものの、資源価格再上昇による新たなコスト増
と、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が重なることで、物価上昇が落ち着くには時間を要するとみられ
る。 
消費者物価への影響にも注意が必要である。10 月は農林水産物が前年比▲25.0％と大幅に下落
した一方、飲食料品は同＋4.8％と、９月の＋4.4％から伸びを高めた。コメ価格の下落が進むな
かでも、加工食品などではコスト高の価格転嫁が続いていることがうかがわれる。コメ価格の下
落は消費者物価の押し下げ要因となるものの、その他の食料品では値上がりが続くとみられ、食
料品全体では年度後半にかけて価格上昇圧力が強まると予想している。

### Page 4

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
4/4
総じてみれば、10 月の企業物価は、これまでのコスト高の価格転嫁が川下で続くなか、資源価
格の再上昇によって川上からの物価上昇圧力も再び強まっていることを示した。今後は新たなコ
スト高が川中・川下へ波及し、価格転嫁の「第二波」が生じる可能性がある。消費者物価につい
ても、こうした企業間取引段階のコスト上昇が時間差を伴って反映されることで、上昇圧力が強
まるとみられる。筆者は、27 年初の消費者物価上昇率が前年比＋３％を上回ると予想している
が、今回の企業物価指数はこうした見通しと整合的な結果だったと考えている。当面、物価の上
振れに警戒が必要な局面が続くだろう。

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC設計‧美國

Nvidia 
(NVDA.O/ NVDA US)

第三季財報預覽：優異的數字恐難激起投資人熱情  
重要訊息 
輝達預計在 11/17 (美國時間)公布第三季財報並召開法說會。 
評論及分析 
超越財測是市場共識。我們預期輝達 FY3Q27 營收可達 1,150 億美元，略高
於公司財測上緣及市場預期的 1,090 億美元，毛利率受記憶體價格影響，微
降至 74%。因設計更改等問題，Rubin 起量從第三季底開始，下游出貨集中
在第四季。放寬 HBM4 的頻寬標準，將加速生產放量，而全年的營收較集中
在第四季。我們預期公司第四季營收指引中位數為 1,250 億美元(+9% 
QoQ)，毛利率受記憶體價格影響下滑到 71%。 
記憶體價格維持高檔，晶片廠商轉嫁勢在必行。我們預期輝達 2026/2027
年資料中心營收將分別達 3,845 億/6,722 億美元。由於 HBM 價格一年一
調，明年初將一口氣反映落後的漲幅，我們預期 HBM4 明年的報價落在 4-5
美元/Gb，對比今年 HBM3e 約 1.7-2.2 美元/Gb。不考慮庫存狀況和折讓，
過去記憶體廠商營收約占 AI 晶片廠商的 15-25%，目前提升至 50%，明年
上看 70%，大幅壓縮晶片廠商的獲利空間。因此，我們預期記憶體升級降
規以及晶片廠商調漲售價轉嫁記憶體報價上漲是勢在必行。我們預估輝達明
年將調漲報價 15%。Vera 除了 VR 系列將新增獨立機櫃需求，目前以訂單
來看，可望較今年成長 150-200%，2027 年貢獻高達 600 億美元的營收，
但供應鏈能否跟上是一個關鍵。目前看法沒有改變。 
市場低估了輝達對於供應鏈的掌握。展望 2027 年，AI ASIC 出貨將首度超
過 GPU，但是 GPU 仍占產值約七成比重。也許基於成長趨緩、資料中心建
置面臨電力和資本諸多限制以及 AI ASIC 蠶食市占率等憂慮，即使連續 15
季財報數字皆超過市場預估和公司財測，公司的評價始終處於歷史低位。對
於評價來說，是否要視輝達為週期股見仁見智，但是我們認為市場遠遠低估
了輝達對於供應鏈的掌握。充沛的現金流更是強勁的護城河，輝達利用融
資、投資和預付款等各種形式扶植供應鏈的擴產，AI ASIC 就算未來在產值
也超越了 GPU，但是未必能夠有足夠的產能支持。 
投資建議 
維持輝達目標價 345 美元，基於 2027 年(2028 財年) 22 倍的本益比，於歷
史均值，目標本益比已反映了營收成長減速。 
投資風險 
AI需求反轉，晶片競爭加劇。公司管理層變動則為系統性風險。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：FY2Q27事業別營收組成 
1993年成立的 Nvidia 是全球 IC 設計大廠，負責設計並銷售積
體電路 (IC) 產品 (如 CPU、GPU 與 DPU)，主要供 PC、伺服器
與汽車產業應用。公司在分離式桌機 GPU 市場居領先地位，
2020 年市佔率接近 80%。受惠於人工智能浪潮，Nvidia 擴大
其加速運算領域優勢，在訓練用 GPU 具有壓倒性的地位，期
望透過人工智慧 (AI) 與高效能運算 (HPC) 推動長期獲利成長。

### Page 5

美國 Nvidia

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

Document 642: 史上最大IPO蓄势待发：Anthropic IPO据称获约六倍超额认购

Source PDF: all report pdf/史上最大IPO蓄势待发：Anthropic IPO据称获约六倍超额认购.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)

### Page 1

此前报道称，认购需求主要来⾃机构投资者。据招股书， Anthropic 计划 IPO 与私募配售合
计募资最⾼ 904 亿美元，公司完全稀释估值约 1.4 万亿美元。
摘要：
Anthropic 的 IPO 投资者需求持续升温。
据彭博社 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇周四最新报道， Anthropic 的⾸次公开募股（ IPO ）已获得约六倍超额认购，
意味着投资者认购需求达到此次发⾏股票数量的约六倍。就在前⼀天，彭博社报道还称，这笔
IPO 已经获得数倍超额认购。最新消息显⽰，随着投资者路演推进，市场认购热情进⼀步升温。
这笔 IPO 有望成为史上规模最⼤的股票上市交易之⼀。据 Anthropic 本周⼆提交的新版招股书，
公司计划以每股 70 ⾄ 78 美元的价格发⾏股票，公开发⾏部分最⾼募资约 643.5 亿美元。此外，公
司还计划通过私募配售向⼤型科技企业募集 260 亿美元，两部分合计募资规模最⾼可达 904 亿美
元，超过此前 SpaceX 创纪录的 IPO 规模。
认购需求达到发⾏规模六倍，机构投资者成为主要推动⼒

据本周三的报道， Anthropic IPO 获得数倍超额认购。报道援引知情⼈⼠称，认购需求主要来⾃
⼤型投资者提交的⼤额意向订单，⽽⾮散⼾投资者推动的短期投机热潮。
仅⼀天之后，彭博社周四的报道进⼀步指出，认购需求已达到发⾏股票数量的约六倍，显⽰这
笔⼤型 IPO 在路演初期就获得了强劲的机构投资者需求。
这也为 Anthropic 后续发⾏定价提供了更⼤的操作空间。此前， Anthropic 公布的 IPO 发⾏价区间
为每股 70 ⾄ 78 美元，但随着投资者需求持续⾛强，承销银⾏已经在考虑提⾼发⾏价。英国《⾦
融时报》此前报道称，银⾏⽅⾯正在考虑将 Anthropic 的 IPO 估值提⾼⾄ 1.5 万亿美元或更⾼。
不过，当前认购倍数仍属于发⾏过程中的需求指标，最终发⾏价格、发⾏规模及认购结果仍可
能发⽣变化。
最⾼募资 904 亿美元， Anthropic 剑指史上最⼤ IPO
Anthropic 此次 IPO 规模极为庞⼤，除了⾯向公开市场发⾏股票，公司还安排了⼤型科技企业参

与的私募配售。
根据提交给美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）的⽂件， Anthropic 计划公开发⾏约 8.25 亿股股票，发
⾏价区间为每股 70 ⾄ 78 美元，按照区间上限计算，公开发⾏部分募资约 643.5 亿美元。
与此同时，公司还计划通过私募配售募集 260 亿美元，投资者包括 AMD 、博通、⾕歌、微软、英
伟达、迈威尔以及台积电等⼤型科技企业。
两部分融资合计最⾼达 904 亿美元。如果顺利完成，这笔交易的规模将超过 SpaceX 此前创纪录
的 IPO ，成为全球资本市场历史上规模最⼤的上市融资交易之⼀。
Anthropic 计划在纳斯达克全球精选市场上市，股票代码为 ANT 。⾼盛和摩根⼠丹利担任此次
IPO 的主承销商，另有 20 家银⾏参与承销。

2 条评论
估值瞄准 1.4 万亿美元， AI 巨头 IPO 接受市场检验
按照此前公布的发⾏⽅案， Anthropic 的完全稀释估值约为 1.4 万亿美元。不过，这⼀估值低于部
分投资者此前的预期。
此前报道称，部分投资者原本期待 Anthropic 获得更⾼估值，甚⾄曾讨论过 1.5 万亿美元以上的⽔
平。随着近期 AI 科技股遭遇抛售，市场对 AI 企业巨额算⼒投⼊、未来盈利能⼒和投资回报的担忧
升温，估值预期也⼀度有所回落。
如今， IPO 获得约六倍超额认购，为 Anthropic 的发⾏前景提供了积极信号，也显⽰⼤型机构投
资者仍愿意为头部 AI 企业的⻓期增⻓前景投⼊巨额资⾦。
不过，强劲的发⾏认购需求并不意味着上市后股价必然上涨。 Anthropic 上市后的表现，还将取

决于投资者对其营收增⻓、盈利前景以及巨额 AI 基础设施投⼊回报的持续评估。
随着 Anthropic 推进上市，这笔超⼤规模 IPO 也将成为检验资本市场 AI 投资热情的重要试⾦⽯。

供應商。近年群聯致力於從傳統的 NAND 儲存元件供應商，轉型為「全方位客製化

➢ 3Q26 AI相關應用營收佔比遽升，毛利率維持相對高檔 
3Q26營收 860.76億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，成長動能來自
於報價上漲以及 AI相關模組出貨量增加所致。公司持續將研發與業務重
心轉向企業級與 AI領域，轉型效益於 3Q26顯著爆發，其營收佔比躍升
至 57%，同步帶動 blended ASP季增逾 30%。獲利表現方面，毛利率
59.7%，較不如先前預期，EPS為 138元，QoQ+16.5%，YoY+1,272.6%。  
➢ 4Q26預估 AI佔比將進一步提升 
展望 4Q26，公司表示 AI應用模組營收佔比已經來到約 65%，顯示企業
級 AI需求動能持續強勁，惟考量 4Q26消費性電子與零售 SSD市場需
求極度低迷，投研部預估 4Q26群聯 blended ASP約季增 12%。預估
4Q26營收約 945.6億元，QoQ+9.9%，YoY+314.8%，毛利率 52.2%，
EPS為 124.15元，QoQ-12.4%，YoY+478.3%。2026年營收約 2,895
億元，YoY+298.4%，毛利率 58.8%，EPS為 449.52元，YoY+1,029.4%。  
➢ 2027年預估出貨量增加，且高附加價值產品營收佔比持續提升 
預估 2027年營收為 3,999.35億元，YoY+38.2%，成長動能來自高附加
價值的 AI記憶體模組營收佔比增加，帶動 ASP平均約季增 3%~5%，
同時隨著 AI應用比例提升，公司所能向原廠爭取的貨源亦有望增加，帶
動出貨量較 2026年成長。此外，公司亦於 2026年拿下三家 NAND原
廠所釋出的 6個 PCIe Gen 5 SSD控制晶片專案，該批控制晶片將相容 
aiDAPTIV架構，效益預計於 2027年逐漸顯現。獲利表現方面，預估報
價漲幅不再會出現強勁成長，取而代之的是高附加價值產品所帶來的獲
利增長，預估 2027年毛利率為 43.95%，EPS為 383.66元，YoY-15.3%。  
➢ 群聯持續積極布局 AI解決方案挹注長期動能，維持買進建議 
評價方面，參考過往記憶體產業上升循環之際，群聯本益比區間約為
3.5X~17X，目前本益比約為 3.9X，看好群聯透過此波記憶體產業上升
循環，加速佈局 AI應用相關解決方案，營運展望正向，惟受到短期市場
雜音影響，以及毛利率開始下滑，恐壓抑短期市場評價，故雖仍維持買
進建議，惟目標價下修至 2,000元(2027F PE 5X)。

個股報告

公司簡介 
群聯電子為全球具備高度市場影響力的 NAND Flash 控制晶片與全方位儲存解決方
案供應商。在市佔率方面，群聯在車用控制器市場的全球份額達到約 40%，而在個人
電腦控制器市場亦具備約 25%市佔率，顯示其在終端應用市場的穩固基礎。近年來，
為降低消費性電子市場景氣循環對營運的衝擊，群聯正式啟動「Phison 3.0」企業轉
型，致力於從傳統的 NAND儲存元件供應商，轉型為「全方位客製化 AI儲存與邊緣
AI 運算平台供應商」。透過將儲存資源與研發量能精準引導至企業級伺服器、車用、
工業控制以及嵌入式 ODM等高附加價值領域。 
圖 1：群聯 2026年 Enterprise SSD營收佔比將急遽提升

資料來源：國票投顧整理 
3Q26 AI相關應用營收佔比遽升，毛利率維持相對高檔 
3Q26營收 860.76億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，營收成長動能主要來自於報
價上漲以及 AI 相關模組出貨量顯著增加所致。公司持續將研發與業務重心轉向高附
加價值的企業級與 AI領域，轉型效益於 3Q26顯著爆發，其營收季增約 88%，佔比
則自 2Q26 38%躍升至 3Q26 57%，同步帶動 blended ASP季增逾 30%，反觀其他
消費性相關應用產品出貨量則在公司策略調整下減少。獲利表現方面，雖然產品組合
轉佳，惟 3Q26 ASP 漲幅放緩，庫存成本提升之下，毛利率修正至 59.7%，QoQ-
5.6ppts，YoY+27.3ppts，較不如先前預期。稅後淨利 305.46億元，QoQ+16.5%，
YoY+1,272.6%，EPS為 138元。 
4Q26預估 AI佔比將進一步提升 
展望 4Q26，公司於法說會上表示 10/2026 AI應用模組營收佔比已經來到約 65%，
且預計將逐月增加直至 2027年底，顯示企業級 AI需求動能持續強勁，惟考量 4Q26 
消費性電子與零售 SSD市場需求極度低迷，預期報價漲幅將進一步趨緩，投研部預估
4Q26群聯 blended ASP約季增 12%。整體而言，預估 4Q26營收約 945.6億元，
QoQ+9.9%，YoY+314.8%，毛利率 52.2%，QoQ-7.5ppts，YoY+10.5ppts，稅後
淨利為 267.66億元，QoQ-12.4%，YoY+478.3%，EPS為 124.15元。 
投研部預估 2026年營收約 2,895億元，YoY+298.4%，毛利率 58.8%，YoY+24.6ppts，
稅後淨利為 987億元，YoY+1,029.4%，EPS為 449.52元。2026年群聯正全面推動
「Phison 3.0」轉型，由傳統 NAND控制器及儲存模組公司轉型為 AI基礎建設及地
端 AI系統解決方案提供商。1H26累計投入的研發費用達 240億元，重心包括 8月

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
發布首款 Gen6企業級 SSD樣品，目前也已進入市場並開始出貨。預估 2026年的研
發費用將進一步擴張以維持其客製化系統方案的技術門檻。觀察當前市況，NAND 
Flash市場的供需結構正經歷由 AI推論所帶動的長期剛性需求增長，而供給方面，原
廠於廠房擴建上仍相對保守，新產能恐於 2027年~2028年方有實際產出。而自需求
端觀察，CSP 在 AI 伺服器基礎建設上投入數千億美元的資本支出，其核心商業模式
仰賴銷售算力以產生實質回報。由於全球使用者透過各類大型語言模型所生成的海量
文字、影像數據均須回傳至雲端伺服器，且 CSP業者為持續優化與訓練次世代 AI模
型，傾向於保留數據，進而衍生出對高密度儲存設備的龐大需求。預估 2027年全球
供需缺口恐逾 20%，原廠將傾向將有限產能優先分配給能創造高附加價值的合作夥
伴，群聯可望藉由掌握車用、工控、Tier-1 CSP等專案需求獲得較佳的貨源保障。 
2027年預估出貨量增加，且高附加價值產品營收佔比持續提升 
展望 2027年及中長期發展，群聯將由純硬體元件供應商轉型為兼具高毛利軟體與系
統服務的整合方案提供者。就產業面來看，考量原廠即便有產能擴增，仍將以
DRAM/HBM為主，NAND供需狀況預期將愈發緊張，且 NVIDIA新一代 Vera Rubin
架構上市後，將會優先吸納市場上大量的 NAND Flash產能，導致其他領域的 NAND
供應出現嚴重短缺，故預期報價將至少維繫於高檔，而群聯亦積極與原廠確保供貨，
同時客戶結構亦朝向工控、AI等面向改善，預期獲利結構得以優化。就公司策略而言，
群聯 2027年的成長動能將由全球擴展以及邊緣端解決方案兩大引擎延續。群聯將在
2H26 至 2027 年全力開拓北美市場、東南亞及印度市場，邊緣端則將迎來大規模導
入期，群聯與聯發科合作將 aiDAPTIV部署於 UFS 4.0手機並與天璣 9500平台整合、
與英特爾的合作專案將於 2027年底前陸續商用上市。由於 DRAM在 2027年依然面
臨短缺與成本過高的痛點，aiDAPTIV解決方案能讓手機或 AI PC僅以 12GB DRAM
或 32GB DRAM即可流暢執行原本需要 24GB LPDDR5或更高規格 DRAM的推論模
型，將高密度 UFS/SSD作為快取，預計將吸引大量的 PC OEM與行動裝置設計導入。
此外，傳統模組業務毛利率易受記憶體價格週期波動，而群聯正透過自研的 AI資料平
台(Phison AI Data Platform, AIDP)建立軟體護城河。AIDP提供包含 Agent平台、
AI資料智慧層、Model/AIOps、資源調度層及硬體層的全棧式架構，專為中小型企業
量身打造低門檻、循序漸進的地端 AI部署方案。目前該方案已在全球取得顯著商業進
展，成功導入 17家銀行機構、85家以上台灣中小型企業、10家以上醫療院所、18
所高等院校及多個政府機關。 
整體而言，預估 2027年營收為 3,999.35億元，YoY+38.2%，成長動能主要來自於
高附加價值的 AI記憶體模組營收佔比增加，帶動 ASP平均約季增 3-5%，同時隨著
AI應用比例提升，公司所能向原廠爭取的貨源亦有望增加，帶動出貨量較 2026年成
長。此外，公司亦於 2026年拿下三家 NAND原廠所釋出的 6個 PCIe Gen 5 SSD控
制晶片專案，該批控制晶片將相容 aiDAPTIV架構，效益預計於 2027年逐漸顯現。
獲利表現方面，預估 2027年報價漲幅不再會出現強勁成長，漲價紅利將逐漸消失，
取而代之的是高附加價值產品所帶來的獲利增長，故即便 2027年毛利率修正，亦將
較往年平均水準高，預估 2027 年毛利率為 43.95%，YoY-14.9ppts，稅後淨利為
836.48億元，YoY-15.3%，EPS為 383.66元。 
群聯持續積極布局 AI解決方案挹注長期動能，維持買進建議 
綜上所述，2026年群聯營運受惠於 NAND Flash原廠報價急漲以及市場嚴重缺貨，
展現極強爆發力，群聯持續專注研發因應後續競爭力的提升，包括多個伺服器 ODM、

### Page 4

- 4 -

個股報告 
CSP的專案導入，加上 Enterprise SSD專案營收佔比放大，創造短期強勁獲利表現。
此外，預估群聯 2027年之後的成長動能，將由 aiDAPTIV+與 PCIe Gen 6企業級控
制器延續。群聯透過簽署 LTA與預付款機制，確保關鍵料件無虞，並且因應 AI PC與
邊緣伺服器對 DRAM容量成本過高的痛點，利用 aiDAPTIV+方案彌補 DRAM運算
需求，預計未來將迎來大規模的 PC OEM=導入，為中長線營運挹注動能。評價方面，
參考過往記憶體產業上升循環之際，群聯本益比區間約為 3.5X~17X，目前本益比約
為 3.9X，看好群聯透過此波記憶體產業上升循環，加速佈局 AI應用相關解決方案，
營運展望正向，同時若以 1H26股利發放率 30%計算，目前殖利率約近 10%，聚力
題材，再加上公司將買回庫藏股 2,000張，隱含當前股價下檔空間相對有限，惟受到
短期市場雜音影響，以及毛利率開始下滑，恐壓抑市場評價，故雖仍維持買進建議，
惟目標價下修至 2,000元(2027F PE 5X)。

Document 692: 托克大举建仓LME铝多头，市场陷入数十年来最严峻逼仓局面

Source PDF: all report pdf/托克大举建仓LME铝多头，市场陷入数十年来最严峻逼仓局面.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

报道称托克集团（ Trafigura ）在伦敦⾦属交易所⼤量买⼊铝，建⽴显著多头头⼨，意图将
⾦属出售⾄亚洲国家套利获利。当前 LME 铝库存降⾄ 1990 年以来最低，多头持仓规模已超
过全部库存，导致市场出现数⼗年来最严峻的现货升⽔。与此同时，亚洲国家铝市场同样
库存紧张，期货价差创 2002 年以来最⾼。
摘要：
报道称托克⼤举买⼊ LME 铝合约，伦敦铝库存创 36 年新低引发历史性逼仓。
11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，据彭博社援引知情⼈⼠透露，托克已在 LME 铝市场积累了⼤量多头头⼨，意在将⾦
属卖往亚洲市场套取利润。
与此同时， LME 铝库存跌⾄ 1990 年以来最低⽔平，仅 226,350 吨，⽽多头持仓规模已超过全部库
存。
周四现货合约相对三个⽉期货的升⽔⼀度飙升⾄每吨 176.80 美元，创逾 30 年来最⾼纪录，令空
头陷⼊被动。
上述局⾯对持有空头头⼨的交易商和制造商造成直接冲击 —— 他们必须在到期前买回合约或实
物交割，⽽市场上可供交割的铝库存已所剩⽆⼏，且⼤部分集中在少数⼤型多头⼿中。

LME 表⽰，交易所已针对⼤额及主导性头⼨设有多项防范机制，并有权要求相关持仓⽅进⾏头
⼨管理。
托克⼤举建仓，成为逼仓核⼼玩家
据彭博获悉的知情⼈⼠描述，托克作为全球最⼤铝交易商之⼀，已在 LME 铝市场建⽴起规模可
观的净多头头⼨。托克发⾔⼈拒绝就此置评。
托克⼤举建仓的背景，是亚洲铝市同步出现的供应紧张。这⼀市场格局为托克等⼤型交易商提
供了套利窗⼝ —— 从 LME 买⼊铝，交割⾄亚洲市场获利。
亚洲市场铝库存近⼏个⽉⼤幅下降，上海期货交易所 12 ⽉交割铝合约较 1 ⽉交割合约升⽔⼀度达
到 550 元 / 吨，为 2002 年以来最陡峭的近⽉逆价差结构。
此外据报道，随着 LME 现货价格飙升，也存在将⾦属直接回交 LME 赚取差价的可能。
LME 数据显⽰，在 11 ⽉主⼒合约，将于下周三到期的近端铝期货中，存在数个⼤型多头头⼨。
尽管以历史绝对规模衡量并⾮异常，但相对于当前极度萎缩的可⽤库存，这些头⼨的⽐重相当
突出。
华尔街⻅闻提及，数据显⽰，截⾄本周⼀，两个实体累计持有的铝权证及现货合约多头，已相

当于 LME 仓库可⽤库存的 60% ⾄ 80% ；⽽ 11 ⽉合约的四个⼤型多头头⼨合计占该合约未平仓量
的⾄少 25% 。
此外，单⼀实体持有 LME 铝权证的⽐例达 30% ⾄ 39% 。

写评论
值得注意的是， LME 现有库存全部为俄罗斯铝，虽在西⽅市场受冷，但在该亚洲国家仍被⼴泛
接受。⾼盛指出，两地市场同时争夺⾦属资源，价差短期内难以缓解。
Wood Mackenzie 分析师 Uday Patel 指出：
Ultima Markets 资深分析师 Elon Gu 表⽰，俄乌冲突爆发后，受西⽅制裁及合规审查影响，欧美
银⾏与下游买家普遍拒收或减持俄铝，导致俄铝在现货市场只能折价抛售，成为国际市场上流
动性最差、估值最低的货源。他表⽰：
规则约束成关键，供需失衡短期难解
⾯对逐步抬头的逼仓⻛险， LME 的市场机制正进⼊考验期。
根据交易所规则，若主导性多头头⼨相对于剩余库存的⽐例持续扩⼤，交易所可强制要求相关
持仓⽅按封顶利率借出头⼨，以缓解市场压⼒。

LME 表⽰，已就铝市场⼤额头⼨问题向相关市场参与者例⾏索取了更多头⼨管理信息。
现货 11 ⽉交割合约相对 12 ⽉合约升⽔约 100 美元，超越了 1997 年以来收盘价数据中的历史纪
录。
尽管来⾃印度尼西亚的铝产量正开始攀升，有望使实物市场逐步转向过剩，但该来源的⾦属⽬
前尚未注册为 LME 可交割品牌，短期内对缓解当前紧张局势作⽤有限。
在此背景下，市场的核⼼问题仍在于：现货溢价还需要升⾄多⾼，才能促使库存持有⽅和多头
持仓⽅将头⼨展期向后滚动。
从某种意义上说， LME 铝价格实际上就是俄罗斯⾦属的价格，因为⽬前仓库⾥只有这⼀种来源
的铝。
这意味着，⼀旦交易进⼊实物交割环节，多头接到的仓单将 100% 是这种 “ 低价值、难转⼿ ” 的俄
铝，这不仅会削弱买⽅的接货意愿，也极易引发盘⾯定价扭曲与流动性⻛险。

Document 700: 政策落地窗口期：地方国有企业参与物流网建设的适配性、发展路径与关键要素

Source PDF: all report pdf/政策落地窗口期：地方国有企业参与物流网建设的适配性、发展路径与关键要素.pdf
Report source: 大公国际资信评估
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

1 / 9 
热点追踪 
领域属性 
国资国企研究 
政策落地窗口期：地方国有企业参与物流网建设的适配
性、发展路径与关键要素 
公用二部|王特佳、翟畅 2026 年 11 月 9 日

摘要： 
在中央政治局会议将物流网建设列入“六张网”建设任务之后，2026 年 8 月，经
国务院同意，国家发展和改革委员会（以下简称“国家发展改革委”）与交通运输部联
合印发《物流网建设实施方案》（以下简称“《方案》”），明确了物流网建设的总体要求
以及目标，我国物流体系的建设进入新阶段。《方案》提出到2030 年的物流网建设目
标正契合地方国有企业的转型需求，为其参与物流网建设提供了宝贵的政策窗口。本
文结合《方案》内容，从角色定位、资源储备、资金来源以及协调能力方面发掘地方
国有企业参与物流网建设的适配性，并提出“平台搭建+参股过渡+实体深耕”，辅以
“金融支撑”的组合路径，最后根据地方国有企业自身特性，提出其参与物流网建设
的关键要素。 
关键词：物流网建设；存量资产盘活；产业服务平台；实体运营商；运营能力

正文： 
一、政策背景 
《方案》提出到 2030 年物流网建设取得明显成效的要求，象征着“通道+枢纽+
网络”物流运行体系的构建逐步从通道与枢纽建设迈向物流网组建，为地方国有企业
提供潜在的转型机遇。 
2018 年，国家发展改革委与交通运输部联合印发《国家物流枢纽布局和建设规
划》，明确提出打造“通道+枢纽+网络”的物流运行体系，开启了物流枢纽从“分散规
划、自发建设”到系统布局、网络化架构的发展道路。此后，国家发展改革委、交通
运输部和国务院办公厅等围绕物流网建设的各细分方向逐步完善政策框架，2026 年 4
月 28 日，中央政治局会议明确要求加强水网、新型电网、算力网、新一代通信网、城
市地下管网、物流网“六张网”规划建设；同年 8 月，国家发展改革委与交通运输部
联合印发《方案》，围绕物流网建设重点环节部署了重点任务，明确了物流网建设的总

### Page 2

热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

2 / 9 
体要求以及目标，也象征着物流网建设完成了从顶层规划到具体行动方案的过渡，进
入具体实施阶段。 
表 1 近年物流相关政策发布情况 
发布日期 发布部门 文件名 重要内容 
2018-12 国家发展改革委、交 通运输部 《国家物流枢纽布局和 建设规划》 打造“通道+枢纽+网络”的物流运行体系 
2019-02 国家发展改革委等 部门 《关于推动物流高质量 发展促进形成强大国内 市场的意见》 构建高质量物流基础设施网络体系 
2022-01 国家发展改革委 《“十四五”现代流通体 系建设规划》 建设国家物流枢纽网络、完善区域物流服务网络、 健全冷链物流设施体系 
2022-05 国务院办公厅 《“十四五”现代物流发 展规划》 
“十四五”现代物流发展目标之一：衔接国家综
合立体交通网主骨架，完成 120 个左右国家物流
枢纽、100 个左右国家骨干冷链物流基地布局建
设，基本形成以国家物流枢纽为核心的骨干物流
基础设施网络 
2024-11 中共中央办公厅、国 务院办公厅 《有效降低全社会物流 成本行动方案》 深入实施国家物流枢纽布局和建设规划，优化国 家物流枢纽布局，系统推进国家物流枢纽建设和 功能提升；完善国家物流枢纽间的合作机制 
2025-11 国家发展改革委等 部门 《关于推动物流数据开 放互联有效降低全社会 物流成本的实施方案》 着力夯实物流数据开放互联基础，依法合规推进 物流公共数据共享开放，促进企业物流数据市场 化流通利用，深化物流与信息流、资金流整合， 打通多式联运数据堵点，优化物流资源配置 
2026-08 国家发展改革委、交 通运输部 《 物 流 网 建 设 实 施 方 案》 
推进物流枢纽建设和互联互通、提升物流通道承
载力、补齐城乡物流设施短板、织密国际物流设
施网络、推动物流设施设备数智化绿色化发展、
提高物流信息共享利用水平、加大政策支持引导 
数据来源：公开资料，大公国际整理 
经过多年的规划探索与发展，我国“通道+枢纽+网络”的物流运行体系已初步形
成，大通道与物流枢纽建设取得阶段性成效，具备较好的设施设备基础，但不同枢纽
间的协调运行、不同运输方式间的快速转换以及产业与设施的协同配套仍存在不足与
待改进之处。《方案》提出到 2030 年，物流网建设取得明显成效，有效实现枢纽与产
业紧密协同、通道与网络内外联通、设施与装备绿色智能、规则与信息全域互通，推
动社会物流总费用与国内生产总值（GDP）的比率降至 13.1%，标志着我国物流运行体
系的构建逐步从通道与枢纽建设迈向物流网组建，重心从设施建设转向运营质效提升，
对于参与主体的能力要求也由“建设”转向“运营”。 
与此同时，随着政企分开改革的不断推进，地方国有企业与地方政府信用绑定逐
步弱化，其政府延伸平台属性持续淡化，自身造血能力的高低影响着未来发展前景，
其市场化转型与存量资产盘活需求日益迫切，物流网建设带来的基础设施需求为地方
国有企业盘活存量资产提供了空间。物流网既是产业发展的基础，也是民生保障的基

### Page 3

热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

3 / 9 
石，其枢纽组网、跨区域协同、应急物流等功能具有一定的网络外部性和准公共品属
性。物流网建设中强调的“畅联通”与“补短板”，需要统筹区域资源与民生保障需要，
单纯依靠市场化主体难以实现最优供给，而地方国有企业兼具资源整合能力与公共服
务职能，在物流网建设中具有较强的功能优势，2026～2030 年《方案》实施期不足 5
年，这为地方国有企业通过参与物流网建设实现产业转型提供了宝贵的政策窗口期。 
二、地方国有企业参与物流网建设的适配性 
地方国有企业在公共性平台承载、存量资产盘活、资金筹措和跨主体协调四方面
具有显著优势，可先作为公共性稳定器与存量资源整合者，待能力具备后再向特定细
分领域的实体运营商延伸，而非一般性物流运营商。地方国有企业参与物流网建设的
适配性具体体现在角色定位、资源储备、资金来源和协调能力四个维度： 
角色定位方面，物流网作为兼具产业支撑性以及民生保障性的新型基础设施，需
同时兼顾产业端的盈利需要以及民生端的公益性需求。纯粹的市场化机制难以完全覆
盖农村物流体系、应急物流设施等收益性较弱而公益性较强且不可或缺的社会服务需
求，需要具有社会服务职能的国有资本承担补位功能，发挥公益性保障作用。同时，
物流网作为面向所有社会群体的基础设施，具有一定的准公共品属性与网络外部性，
部分环节存在自然垄断特征，若完全由市场化主体主导，可能因追求垄断租金而削弱
网络的开放接入和区域公平，需要国有资本发挥稳定器作用，以弥补市场失灵为限，
不与市场化主体争夺竞争性业务。 
资源储备方面，地方国有企业参与物流网建设既有客观基础，也有内在动力。客
观层面，地方国有企业往往具有属地资源优势且可承接地方政府事权对应的相关业务，
获得业务端的倾斜支持，如土地资源：多数地方城投、交投类企业持有仓储、货场、
厂房等存量土地，在解决物流网运营场所问题上具有存量空间优势；如供水、供热、
交通基础设施运营权等。主观层面，地方国有企业当前存在较强的资产盘活需求，物
流网的建设运营为其提供了理想的场景。 
资金来源方面，地方国有企业凭借其主体资质和信用条件，对于部分仓储物流基
础设施建设，在符合资金投向与项目管理要求的前提下，能够争取中央预算内资金、
地方政府专项债、政策性金融工具等方面的资金，具有一定渠道优势；同时，作为园
区、厂房等基础设施的投资建设主体和产权主体，在资产权属清晰、现金流稳定的前
提下，地方国有企业可通过资产证券化拓宽融资渠道，同时若符合基础设施 REITs 对
项目类型、运营年限、收益率等方面的要求，也可以通过基础设施 REITs 等工具盘活

### Page 4

热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

4 / 9 
存量资产。但需注意，项目的开发建设需要严守不新增地方政府隐性债务、不依托政
府信用为项目融资提供担保的政策红线。 
协调能力方面，地方国有企业具备协调政府与市场、整合多方资源的能力，能够
作为地区资源整合链接主体发挥作用：一是地方国有企业承接上层政策的落地实施，
同时能够将市场主体的实际反应反馈至政策制定者，是政府与市场间的沟通桥梁；二
是地方国有企业能够协调交通运输主体、城市建设规划主体和市场化运营主体等多方
资源与利益，合理规划物流设施布局，打通从干线到枢纽的“最后一公里”，提升货物
从运输大通道进入枢纽这一运转流程的效率。 
三、地方国有企业参与物流网建设的发展路径 
随着物流运行体系建设重心的转变，地方国有企业参与物流网建设发展的路径，
可以由单一的产业服务平台建设，转向平台能力与实体运营的深度融合。依据资源禀
赋和区域条件，地方国有企业可构建“平台搭建+参股过渡+实体深耕”的组合路径，
深度参与物流网的建设发展，同时探索“金融支撑”路径，为物流网建设进行金融赋能。 
（一）平台搭建：以物流产业服务平台为切入点，盘活存量设施资源 
物流业具备一定的重资产属性，对于转运设施、场地、仓储等有天然的需求，这
一需求适配地方国有企业盘活存量闲置土地、厂房等资产的需要以及其协调区域不同
主体存量资源的能力，为地方国有企业参与物流网建设提供了理想的切入口。对于运
输条件较好、产业需求较高的物流枢纽或制造业发达地区，地方国有企业可充分发挥
其协调政府与市场、整合多方资源的能力，整合区域内物流资源及需求，通过盘活存
量闲置土地、厂房等资产，新建或改扩建大型物流园区，借助规模化效应降低货物调
配、仓储等成本，做大货物吞吐量以争取铁路专用线、短支航道或管道接入，从而提
升货物转运效率，进一步降低物流成本。 
江西省萍乡市湘东区赣西国际港项目为上述路径提供了理想的范本，2025 年，江
西省萍乡市湘东区赣西国际港至福州江阴港的海铁联运定点专列正式投入运营，显著
提升了区域物流运转效率，其“赣西国际港公路港”项目采用“政府统筹、国企实施、
市场运作”三级联动开发机制，通过整合破产企业用地、低效工业用地和集体土地三
类产权分散地块，实现了连片开发与规模运作，预计可为湘东工业园企业降低 20%综
合物流成本，节约40%铁路运输时间及成本。 
对于货物吞吐量较小的区域，地方国有企业需避免脱离地区产业条件的盲目同质

### Page 5

TUbQYoVpedtcJWcEyS1mLIvozTdpmNO9x8AJgfZJPghAZJH35wv8heS7Y33WtTm4 
                      热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

5 / 9 
化建设，可着重发挥跨区域协调能力，重点依托物流枢纽城市，会同区域内平台企业、
枢纽运营主体等，完善货源组织、运作流程、信息联通等协同运作机制和服务标准规
范，推动建立区域物流信息共享平台，降低物流网运行中的交易成本。 
（二）参股过渡：以实物出资或产业基金切入，由服务平台向运营商平稳过渡 
地方国有企业可通过实物出资或产业基金等方式参股物流企业，既能在实现存量
资产盘活的同时解决物流业市场化运营主体的生产要素需求，又能通过参与合规设立
的市级以及省级产业基金发挥带头引领作用，发挥国有资本的引导带动作用，与民营
资本形成互补并获取物流业发展红利，降低地方国有企业进入物流网建设发展的门槛。
此外，通过参股物流企业，地方国有企业能够在一定程度上更深入地接触物流业务实
际运营，为后续实现产业转型积累经验，因而是从产业服务平台向物流业运营商转型
的良好过渡路径。 
深圳市市属企业深圳国际控股有限公司采取“自营+合作”的双轨模式，通过与冷
链龙头万纬物流发展集团有限公司合资成立运营公司，提升区域物流建筑标准及冷链
管理水平，为地方国有企业通过参股合资方式积累经验，提供了可供参考的样本。 
（三）实体深耕：以混合所有制且聚焦场所型细分业务，深度参与运营 
深度参与物流业务实际运营、成为物流业实体运营商，意味着向产业主体实质性
转型，但同时也意味着更大的风险。在参与物流网建设、推进产业转型的初期，地方
国有企业可采取混合所有制形式，按国资监管要求履行决策程序，从参股逐步过渡至
控股，结合市场化主体的运营经验及地方国有企业的资源禀赋，降低转型初期的运营
风险；在业务选择方面，可优先聚焦固定场所细分物流业务，包括货场、联运场、通
用仓储等运营业务，以及冷链物流、流通加工（含辐照、预制加工等）等产业配套运
营业务，避免贸然介入干线运输、终端配送等区域跨度大、经验需求高的业务环节。 
金台铁路临海东站货站（物流仓储中心）由临海市城市产业发展集团有限公司负
责建设和运营，2024 年 5 月进入试运营，是第三批国家级示范工程——台州湾区公铁
水多式联运示范工程的七个项目之一，可提供铁路运输组织、中转换装、装卸储存、
加工包装等综合服务，预计初期发运量可达 155 万吨，远期可达223 万吨，为提升区
域物流转运水平提供了助力，为地方国有企业直接参与产业链、做物流业实体运营商
提供了可观察样本。 
此外，当前农村物流设施、重要物资仓储设施以及应急物流设施等存在一定短板，

### Page 6

热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

6 / 9 
《方案》提出推进下沉市场冷链设施建设、完善农产品批发市场和农贸市场冷链物流
设施等方向。地方国有企业可依托闲置村镇客运站、邮政营业场所、供销社网点等资
源，以轻资产改造为主，建立村镇农产品加工、冷链仓储及流通中心，完善农村物流
体系、服务区域农业发展的同时盘活存量资源，响应“十五五”规划相关部署，加快
农业农村现代化建设。 
（四）金融支撑：以设备租赁与创新金融模式，优化要素供给 
《方案》提出“优化要素供给”。传统的厂房、土地租赁或实物注资是地方国有企
业对物流业以及物流企业提供生产要素的常见方式，而金融途径同样存在一定业务发
掘空间。物流网建设发展离不开运输，运输设备对于运营主体而言是一笔较大的资金
投入。借鉴航空业成熟的融资租赁以及经营租赁模式，将其运用于其他运输设备，在
减轻运营主体资本投入的同时提高运输设备流转效率，是一个潜在可行的思考方向。 
2026 年，成都产融服务控股集团有限公司下属成都工投融资租赁有限公司通过融
资租赁的方式为某新能源商用车运营企业提供资金支持用以购置新能源重卡，该企业
获得车辆资产后，以经营性租赁方式出租给物流运输企业，反映出融资租赁正加快向
物流运输环节渗透，通过专业化、市场化的租赁服务，让运输企业无需承担大额购车
成本，可视为金融业务模式的创新已开始向物流业拓展的一个信号。但需注意，设备
租赁需要一定的资金投入，会带来一定的资金压力，同时涉及设备残值风险、承租人
违约风险、设备处置风险以及再出租风险等，需要经营主体具有一定的业务运营能力
以及风险把控能力。 
四、地方国有企业参与物流网建设的关键要素 
（一）找准定位：参与的前提 
地方国有企业参与物流网建设，首先需找准自身定位。从产业链角色看，地方国
有企业可根据地区产业结构、交通运输条件、同业竞争压力等资源禀赋判断自身是否
具备向实体运营商转变的条件，找准在物流网建设中能够扮演的角色；也可根据自身
经验、人才储备、治理架构等能力因素判断转型的时机，在不同的发展阶段匹配合适
的角色定位。根据中国物流与采购联合会于 2024 年发布的《第七次全国物流园区（基
地）调查报告》，受调查对象中 38.3%的物流园区反映同质化竞争制约发展，因此无论
是作为服务平台还是运营商，均需要根据区域条件进行差异化布局，避免同质化竞争
与资源浪费。

### Page 7

热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

7 / 9 
从区域网络位置看，区域物流枢纽通过交通干线组成物流网的“主动脉”，更为分
散的物流中心通过交通分支与区域物流枢纽相连，构成物流网细密的“毛细血管”。物
流枢纽城市的国有企业可积极争取区域物流网构建的主导者角色，在地方政府统筹下，
从设施建设、参与规则标准制定、区域协同等方面规划区域物流网的建设发展；处于
各节点物流中心的国有企业则需根据地区禀赋进行规划，更多以参与者角色为区域物
流网建设添砖加瓦，通过以点带面推动区域物流网构建。 
（二）提升运营能力：参与的支撑 
物流网构建的重心在运营。无论是作为服务平台还是实体运营商，地方国有企业
具备足够的运营能力才可支撑其深度参与物流网的建设。首先，地方国有企业可转变
经营思维，弱化甚至摒弃长期以来单纯以完成建设任务为导向的经营逻辑，加强对运
营效果、效率的关注。其次，地方国有企业可基于以运营成效为主的经营思维，构建
符合物流业经营逻辑的绩效评价体系，提高周转率等效率效果指标与空驶率、单位成
本等成本指标的考核权重。此外，地方国有企业还可通过借鉴行业领先企业经验、引
进和培养行业人才等方式增强能力储备，形成符合自身条件的业务经营模式，以提升
业务拓展与风险应对水平。 
（三）严控增量、防范风险：参与的底线 
地方国有企业经过长期的建设发展，体系内积累了大量的空置厂房、园区、土地
等低效甚至无效资产。当前地方国有企业的首要任务是盘活积压的存量资产，实现资
产资源的变现，需审视企业自身资产以及债务情况，谨慎进行举债新增增量资产的运
作，以防债务压力进一步加大、现金流状况进一步恶化、经营风险进一步加深，造成
国有资产流失。同时，需高度关注参与物流网建设过程中的合规风险，警惕项目建设
及运营过程中新增隐性债务风险，对于自主开发建设的项目严格遵循自主融资、自主
运营、自负盈亏的市场化运营机制。 
五、总结 
物流网是连接全国市场的新型基础设施，需要多方共同参与建设运营。地方国有
企业在角色定位、资源储备、资金来源以及协调能力方面与具有与物流网建设需求的
适配性，能够在其中扮演重要角色。同时，《方案》为地方国有企业参与物流网建设提
供了宝贵的窗口期，可作为地方国有企业参与物流网建设的时间规划参考：2026～
2027 年可作为地方国有企业参与物流网建设的项目谋划实施与政策争取阶段，2028～

### Page 8

热点追踪 | 国资国企研究

8 / 9 
2030 年可作为参与深化以及能力强化发展阶段，以期推动完成 2030 年物流网建设取
得明显成效的《方案》目标，而具体路径方面可以物流产业服务平台为切入点，通过
参股方式积累经验并培育团队，最终实现向产业运营主体的转变。但地方国有企业需
注意参与过程的循序渐进，正确找准定位，认识自身运营能力方面的短板并着重提高，
同时严格控制债务及项目新增，防范经营及合规风险。

的低水準に落ち込んでいる。住宅着工件数も水準を切り下げている。ちなみに、期間30年の

ず、10年間でも14.3％しか減らない（元金均等返済の場合は５年毎に当初元本の16.7％が減
少）。

・そして家賃以外のサービス価格に重大な影響を与える労働コストについては、雇用統計で示
される平均時給を見る限り、反転上昇は予想しにくい。ＮＦＩＢ中小企業調査によれば、３
ヶ月先の人件費計画を問う項目の数値は過去２年程度の下限付近で推移している。すなわち
現在のインフレは、財価格が関税と原油高によって押し上げられている一方、サービス価格
は落ち着いている、という整理になる。

3/4 
・こうした状況下、Ｆｅｄ中枢のジェファーソン副議長、ウォラー理事、ウィリアムズ・ＮＹ
連銀総裁は、９月ＦＯＭＣ以降、連続利上げを急がない姿勢を示している。このうちウィリ
アムズ総裁は、年内にあと１回の利上げが適切となる可能性を指摘する一方、現時点では
2027年に追加利上げが必要になるとは考えていないとの見解を示している。

・次にＥＣＢ理事会に目を向けると、こちらも政策金利の据え置きが広く予想されている。中
銀預金金利は2.5％となろう。ＯＩＳ金利から逆算した10月の利上げ確率は４％弱と低いもの
の、12月理事会では90％程度と極めて高い。ユーロ圏の９月ＣＰＩは前年比＋3.8％と８月か
ら0.6％pt加速、コアＣＰＩは同＋2.5％と過去２年程度のレンジ内に収まっているものの、
エネルギー価格高騰が賃金上昇を通じてサービス価格に波及することを予防する意味もあ
り、筆者も12月は利上げに踏み切ると予想している。労働者（組合）の交渉力が強いユーロ
圏では、インフレの二次的波及が起こりやすい。

・そうした特性を象徴するのが、2011年の利上げである。当時は欧州債務危機の最中で、金融
市場の緊張感は高まっていたが、「アラブの春」と言われる中東・北アフリカ情勢の緊迫化
を背景とする原油高が、ユーロ圏のインフレを加速させ得るとの判断からトリシェ総裁（当
時）は利上げを断行した。その後のドラギ体制ではデフレの脅威と闘ったこともあってＦｅ
ｄに近い考え方を採用し積極的な金融緩和を実施していたが、コロナ期のインフレを経て再
び物価に敏感な姿勢に回帰している。

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
・そして日銀の金融政策決定会合も、政策金利の据え置きが広く予想されている。そのうえで
筆者は①高田委員と田村委員の投票行動、②浅田委員と佐藤委員の物価認識に注目してい
る。

・高田委員は、政策金利の据え置きに対して１月、３月、４月、７月の４回の会合で反対票を
投じており、今回も利上げを主張するとみられる。田村委員は４月の据え置きに際しては利
上げを主張して反対票を投じたものの、７月は賛成票を投じており、今回どう動くかは興味
深い。仮に田村委員も利上げを主張するならば、次回以降の利上げ観測を強める要因になろ
う。

・浅田委員と佐藤委員が、基調的な物価上昇率が２％に到達したとの認識に対して異論を唱え
る可能性がある。両委員は、日本経済の足腰の弱さを理由に９月会合で利上げに反対票を投
じた経緯があり、今回もハト派色を前面に押し出す可能性がある。仮にそうした主張をすれ
ば、円安を誘発し得る。一方で、基調的な物価上昇率の２％到達を認める場合は、９月下旬
頃から相次いでいる高市首相、片山財務相、木内経済財政相の「脱リフレ」に同調したとの
見方が広がるのではないか。そうなれば円高を促し得る。
